# App Context Changelog

Newest first. One entry per merged change that alters behavior, schema, process or backups. Keep entries short; the detail lives in the module dossier and the PR.

Format: `YYYY-MM-DD · PR · what · migrations (version → apply state)`

## 2026-11-13 — Reviews: the leaderboard gets an on/off switch in Control Center, starts hidden, and moves to the bottom of the page (PR #739, squash-merged 2026-11-13 as `a4750ab7` at 11:14 IST, deployed 11:16 IST) · `20270702000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-13 by the owner, after a verified backup (11:33 IST)
- **Owner ask.** The team had started seeing the ranking and was asking for its parameters. Hide it for everyone by default, with an option in Control Center; when shown, it must not lead the page and needs a better design.
- **Built.** Control Center > Reviews > Rankings (one switch). Off: the Reviews landing panel, the "Review leaderboard" button on BOE Credits and the Past months page all hide (the page says the ranking is switched off). On: the board sits LAST on `/customer-reviews`, with rank badges, a bar per person against the leader, and a "How is this ranked?" note.
- **Fails closed.** The screens treat loading, a failed read, a missing row or a missing table as hidden, so the code can deploy before the migration is applied and the ranking is already hidden.
- **Database.** One table (`review_display_settings`, one row, starts `false`) and one admin-only function (`set_review_rankings_visible`). `customer_review_leaderboard()` is not touched, so hiding is a display rule only.
- **Proof.** PGlite rehearsal of the migration (19 checks: applies twice, one row, starts hidden, member/deleted/inactive admin/signed-out refused, no direct client write, re-apply keeps the choice); `rankingsSwitch.test.ts`; `navModel.test.ts`.
- **Applied.** The owner ran the backup, a rolled-back rehearsal on the live database ("REHEARSAL OK", nothing kept), then the migration with `supabase db query --linked --file` and `supabase migration repair 20270702000000 --status applied`. Read-only check afterwards: one row, `rankings_visible = false`, `anon` cannot execute the function, `authenticated` can execute it and cannot write the table; `migration list` shows `20270702000000` in `remote`. The ranking has NOT been switched on: it stays hidden until an administrator turns it on in Control Center > Reviews > Rankings. Not seen signed in.
- **Trap found while applying.** The live migration history has no row for `20270624000000`, `20270625000000`, `20270626000000`, `20270627000000` and `20270628000000` (applied by hand on 2026-11-13 without a repair). `supabase db push` would try to run them again, so never use it until they are repaired; use `db query --file` + `migration repair` as above.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR, then `drop function public.set_review_rankings_visible(boolean); drop table public.review_display_settings;` (header of the migration).

## 2026-11-13 — BOE Scoreboard, slice 2: the team vote and the owner's rating (PR #726, squash-merged 2026-11-13 as `70acf17`) · `20270626000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-13 by the owner, before the merge (owner's word; not re-checked from the cloud session)

- **Built.**

  - Both close at 23:00 IST on the last day. The vote counts in the month's final record.
- **Database.** `scoreboard_votes` (not self, at most 3 a voter, never changed) and `scoreboard_ratings`, both closed to the browser.

## 2026-11-12 — Confirmed Payments: search by any order number, the order's confirmed total, correct an old order's client (PR #719, squash-merged 2026-11-13 as `ebfc7df8`, deployed 08:39 IST; PR 2 of 2, built on PR 1) · `20270701000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-13 alone (db query --file + migration repair), after a verified backup and a rolled-back rehearsal
- **Owner ask.** Search should find a payment by the order number or PI number it is recorded against and, once it shows the result, show the total confirmed amount of that order; and the client of an old order must be addable and editable.
- **Built.**
  - **Search** also matches `order_search_text` (a computed field on `finance_received_payments`): every Order number, PI Draft reference or reserved number, the PI an Order came from, and old order number and client of the payment's active allocations. Before, only the one Order the projection names was searchable.
  - **Order total banner** above the list when the term (3 characters or more) names an order: `Order 0425 · ₹12,50,000.00 confirmed · 3 payments`, from `finance_order_confirmed_totals`. It is the money allocated to the order on every page, ignoring the date and status filters, and never the sum of the visible Amount cells (a split payment would count twice). Several matches: up to five, with a note to type the full number.
  - **Old order client:** Edit Received Payment lists each old order of the payment with its client; a change calls `correct_legacy_order_client` (`finance.manage`), is recorded on the payment's history (new event `allocation_legacy_client_corrected`), and never writes the payment row, so it works while a cash handover locks the payment.
- **Database.** Three functions; the allocation guard restated (20270407 §2 plus a marker clause for the client name of one allocation); the activity CHECK widened by one event. No table, no row, no amount changes.
- **Numbering and apply (13 Nov).** Numbered `20270624000000` first (taken by #721 while this was open), then `20270626000000` (Scoreboard slice 2, #726), then `20270627000000` (Scoreboard slice 3, #727); now `20270701000000`, deliberately clear of the Scoreboard and Quotations sequence (`28` and `29` are claimed by open PRs). Applied to the live database ALONE (`db query --file`, then `migration repair --status applied`), after a verified backup, a rolled-back rehearsal as a real admin and a dry-run; a read-only check as a real admin passed afterwards. It was applied while still numbered `20270626000000`; its ledger row was moved to `27` and finally to `20270701000000` (two `migration repair` commands each time), so the Scoreboard files `26` and `27` are not hidden from a later `db push`. The push of both pending migrations had failed: Day Close's `20270624000000` stops on its own dependency check because the live database has no Day Close tables (the ledger rows for 22 and 23 both belong to quotation versions), and the Scoreboard tables are missing too. A plain `db push` now needs `--include-all` for `24` to `27`.
- **Proof.** `confirmed_payments_order_search_pglite.mjs` runs the real migration in a throwaway Postgres: 38 checks, and 14 deliberate breaks of the migration each make it fail. Finance, orders, navigation, Day Close, Scoreboard and hooks tests, 0 failures; `tsc`; eslint on the changed files.
- **Not proved.** PostgREST accepting the computed field inside the search filter was proved on PGlite only, not on the live PostgREST. Not seen signed in. No browser test (owner's rule).
- **Rollback.** In the migration header: drop the three functions, re-emit the 20270407 guard; the event value may stay in the CHECK. Code first reverts cleanly (the search falls back by reverting the one column).
## 2026-11-12 — Confirmed Payments: Order / PI column, status as a small icon, old-order client shown (PR #718, squash-merged 2026-11-13 as `d937dae8`, deployed 06:24 IST) · no SQL
- **Owner ask.** The Confirmed Payments list should show the Order number / PI number each payment was recorded against, several for a split payment; the Allocation status should shrink to small icons; the client of an old order was not shown ("Not identified"). PR 2 (migration) adds search by order number with a confirmed total and editing an old order's client.
- **Built.**
  - New **Order / PI** column after Client (190px): one line per Order, PI Draft (reference and reserved number) or old order with its own amount; links where the reader may open the record. No new query: it reads the complete allocation targets the page already loads once per page.
  - **Allocation status** is one small icon (column 156px to 48px): ring, half, tick, "!" in the pill colours, words in the tooltip and accessible name. Phone cards keep the word pill.
  - **Old-order client:** a payment whose money is only on old orders of one client shows that client (display only; no write).
  - The table is about 80px wider: `CONFIRMED_TABLE_MIN_CONTAINER_PX` 1045 to 1125. A 1366px laptop with the sidebar pinned open now gets cards (the hover rail still gets the table).
- **Proof.** `tsc`; eslint on the changed files; 3,203 finance, navigation and UI tests, 0 failures (offline env). `paymentSurfaces.test.ts`, `allocatedAgainst.render.test.tsx` and `allocatedAgainst.test.ts` updated and extended.
- **Not seen signed in.** No browser test (owner's rule).
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR; nothing in the database changed.

## 2026-11-12 — Payroll and the My pages read your own row once (PR open, branch `perf/context-reuses-profile`) · no SQL
- **Owner ask.** "Yes, fix it", after the live recount of #711/#712 found one repeat left.
- **The repeat.** On Payroll, My Attendance, My Credits and My Payroll the page is not behind a ModuleGuard, so it reads the 8-column row first. The shell's `usePermissionContext` started about 1.2 s later and always read the identical row again: 4 own-row reads instead of 3.
- **Changed.** New `profileForFirstResolve(qc, userId)`: on the context's FIRST resolve, a profile row read in the last 30 s (and not marked stale) is reused instead of read again. A refetch always reads, so a role change still reaches an open page within 30 s. A null row (failed or missing) is never reused, so a failed read still denies.
- **Tests.** Three new checks in `sharedReads.test.ts` (reused on the first resolve; a refetch reads; missing, null, older than 30 s or stale reads). Full suite: 20,687 tests, 0 failed, 9 skipped; `tsc` and eslint clean.

## 2026-11-12 — Day Close moves into Task Management; proof on every finished task (PR #721) · `20270624000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-12 by the owner, before the merge (owner's word; not re-checked from the cloud session)

  - proof on every task a member finishes;
  - "Close my day" also files the EOD log;
  - Team Today goes inside Task Management.
- **Changed.**

  - Team Today moved to `/tasks/team-today` (admin sidebar).
  - Removed: `/my-day`, its nav entry, `/attendance/day-close`, `/api/day-close/proof`.

- **Proof.**
  - `day_close_in_tasks_pglite.mjs`: 10 checks.
  - `rules.test.ts` rewritten; `leaveLock.test.ts`.
  - 1,400 tests across tasks, layout, modules and dashboard; `tsc` and eslint.

## 2026-11-12 — Orders: a PI sent back to the salesperson shows the status "Returned to Sales" (PR #716, squash-merged 2026-11-12 23:12 IST as `8eb156e4`, deployed 23:14 IST) · no migration
- **Owner ask.** "Whenever I send it back to the salesperson it does not change the status": it should show, to the approver and the sales team, that the PI is with Sales and that they must make the requested changes and resubmit. Plan approved the same evening ("Yes, build it"); the salesperson edits through Edit PI.
- **Changed.** While the newest send-back stands (`salesReturnStage`), the Order banner reads "PI V<n> returned to Sales" (amber) with the instruction first ("The approved PI cannot be edited in place … Edit PI … resubmits; the revised PI goes to Operations once it is approved"), then Operations' reason and the sent line; the ERP card reads "Returned to Sales for changes."; once a revised PI waits for approval it reads "… revised PI waiting for approval". The salesperson's flagged-PI card reads "Returned to you: make the changes and resubmit PI V<n> with Edit PI"; the approver's Needs your decision row turns into "Returned to Sales: waiting for the revised PI" with the comment (no longer a red decision).
- **Derived, not stored.** From the send-back history entry, the handoff and the pending revision. It ends by itself when Operations flags again or accepts, when the PI's owner answers Operations after it, or when the revised PI is approved (new version). No database change. Not built: locking Operations' buttons, a list column, a stored status.
- **Proof.** New `returnedToSales.test.tsx`; three pins updated (ERP line, card wording, the shared history read); `tsc`, eslint, full `npm test`, `next build`. Not seen signed in or on a phone.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR.

## 2026-11-12 — Day Close: plan, proof, close before punch-out, strikes, leave lock (branch `claude/boe1-employee-monitoring-t5ypei`) · `20270622000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-12 by the owner, before the merge
- **Owner ask.** Staff waste about half the day, with no defined work and no accountability; leave is taken at will. Wanted from tomorrow: today's work is defined, finished or explained before going home, and leave is blocked while work is pending. Named: Dhruv, Rakesh (social media), the SEO person, sales, Jasvi ("all of team").
- **Decisions.** Strikes only (no money for now). Plan due 10:30, closing opens 18:00, office 10:00–18:30, Monday to Saturday. Leave with work pending is blocked except by the owner. The owner alone reviews.
- **Built.**
  - `/my-day`: the plan, items closed with a link or photo or a reason, and close the day.
  - `/attendance/day-close`, Team Today: punches, plans, proof, calls logged, overdue tasks, strikes; who takes part; the reviewer seat.
  - A Day strip on `/modules`.
  - A nightly check (Vercel cron 23:15 IST).
  - A leave lock in the attendance-request handlers, with a 🔒 mark on the queue.

## 2026-11-12 — Permissions looked up once in Meetings, Review Workflow, Sample Tracking and Assets (PR #712, squash-merged 2026-11-12 22:13 IST as `3e26083e`, deployed 22:16 IST) · no SQL
- **Owner ask.** "check other modules for duplicate requests too", then "Yes, build both". This is PR 2 of 2; PR 1 (#711) reads your own row once.
- **Measured live before.** Each module's gate already loads the profile and the permissions; these screens loaded them again.
  - Review Workflow: the profile 3 more times and the permissions 3 more. The gate asked twice, and the landing page started `useCustomerReviews` twice.
  - Meetings: the role read twice, and the permissions looked up by its hand-written gate.
  - Assets & Access: the profile once more and the permissions twice more (start-up, then an immediate re-resolve of the same view).
  - Sample Tracking: the profile once more and the permissions once more.
- **Changed.**
  - The Meetings and Review Workflow gates read the signed-in person's permission context (`usePermissionContext`: the profile plus one `resolve_effective_permissions_for_user` call for every module), as the Orders gate has since `202cbaf8`.
  - Same rules. Meetings: admin, or `view`, or `manage`. Review Workflow: an active account with `use` or `verify`, no admin short-circuit. A failed profile read or a failed permission lookup still refuses.
  - New `cachedModulePermissions(qc, userId, moduleKey)` returns the gate's answer for one module only while it is under 30 seconds old; otherwise the caller asks the database as before. `useMeetings`, `useCustomerReviews`, the Samples page and the Assets page use it on first load. Focus refreshes still ask the database.
  - The Review Workflow landing page renders `CustomReviewsScreen` directly instead of running `useCustomerReviews` a second time only to wait for it.
  - Assets does not re-resolve the view it was just opened on; switching views later still re-resolves.
  - The four screens read the profile through `profileQueryOptions`. Assets used to add `position`, which nothing on the page read.
- **Tests.**
  - New: three checks of `cachedModulePermissions` in `sharedReads.test.ts` (a fresh copy; an empty map is no grant; stale or missing asks again).
  - Restated to the new code with the same rules: `adminBypass` (the gate reads no role; a failed resolve is an empty map), `testCardPurge`, `customerReviewOutreach` (the route guard), `enforcement` (Meetings still resolves permissions).
  - Full suite: 20,640 tests, 0 failed, 9 skipped. `tsc` and eslint are clean.
- **Live recount 2026-11-12 ~22:30 IST** (production `3e26083e`, full page loads in Chrome, counted once the page was quiet for 3 s). Every page reads your own row 3 times (profile, role, designation) and looks permissions up once (`_for_user`), with 0 exact repeats and 0 failed requests.
  - Meetings 7 requests (before: the role read twice and a separate lookup).
  - Review Workflow 13 (before: the profile 3 more times and 3 more lookups). Its three `customer_review_custom_submissions` reads are three different queries, not a repeat.
  - Sample Tracking 15, Assets & Access 13.

## 2026-11-12 — Console guard tightened so the "preloaded but not used" warnings cannot come back (PR #693, squash-merged 2026-11-12 15:45 IST as `46adaca3`) · no SQL
- **Why.** The owner asked: "Is there anything which can generate similar kind of errors in future then do work on it and solve it". A read-only sweep of about 38 pages in the owner's Chrome found:
  - pages covered: every module home, plus the Orders, Finance, Tasks and Sales sub-pages;
  - 0 console errors or warnings from BOE, 0 failed requests and 0 unused stylesheets;
  - what remained came from outside BOE: a Chrome extension; the Vercel Toolbar, which the owner sees because of the `__vercel_toolbar` cookie; and the owner's network stalling some page loads 6-19 s before any request left Chrome.
- **Changed (test only).** `noBackgroundPrefetch.test.ts` now also fails on:
  - `next/link` outside AppLink when written with double quotes or loaded by `import()`/`require()`;
  - any `next/form`;
  - `router.prefetch?.(`;
  - any hand-written preload: `rel="preload|prefetch|modulepreload"`, React `preload()`/`preinit()`.
- **Checks.** A sixth case proves the patterns catch each spelling and leave `prefetchQuery`, `preconnect()` and `rel="stylesheet"` alone. A deliberately bad file made 4 of the 6 cases fail; the old guard would have passed it.
- **Not changed.** Polled refreshes (For you and handovers, every 60 s) already retry once (`retry: 1` in Providers). A dropped connection therefore adds at most two failed-request lines per refresh, so they are left as they are.

## 2026-11-12 — Tasks: automatic escalation removal applied (PR #652, merged 2026-11-11 as `8ff4dafd`) · `20270610000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-12 13:00 IST
- **Before.** The owner said yes after the read-only check in the PR read, on production: one hourly job `task-health-check` (`0 * * * *`, active), function writes escalation lines `true`, notifications `false`, stale flag `true`, 1,031 old `escalated` lines on 900 tasks. The live function was `SECURITY INVOKER`, no `SET`, owner `postgres`, so `CREATE OR REPLACE` changes no property. Its body equals `20261015000000` except CRLF line endings, so the planned rollback stands; the exact live text was also saved outside the repo.

## 2026-11-12 — Pages load on click: no page is loaded in advance, anywhere (branch `perf/load-on-click`) · no migration
- **Owner decision.** "Yes, load on click" (2026-11-12), after the console kept filling with "preloaded but not used" on every page ("this has really become too common").
- **Why the earlier fixes could not end it.** The owner's screenshots held 72 and then 173 warnings but only 2-3 distinct files (Control Center `0mn.s1bn975w.css`, Orders `0vpo57o72-4do.css`, Attendance & Payroll `0-se8gau-3wku.css`, Pricing's `01yo0_929s3t8.css`, the Dashboard's `0w~i7isy3ru4c.css`). Chrome re-reports every still-unused preloaded file as the person moves around a long-open tab, so any page loaded in advance and not opened (a For-you card, a menu item, a card the pointer rested on) multiplies. #649, #651, #656, #658 and #659 narrowed WHEN pages loaded; this removes it.
- **Changed.** New `src/components/ui/AppLink.tsx` (`next/link` with `prefetch={false}` forced after the caller's props). All 113 files that imported `next/link` now import `AppLink as Link` (one line each, by script; nothing else in them changed). Every `router.prefetch` is gone: Dashboard (`/tasks/my`, `/notifications` on mount), Modules cards (250 ms rest), My Tasks and Assigned By Me rows, the shell's Home button, the Orders/Finance switch, the Finance/Orders menus and rail Home link, Quick Actions, Notifications task titles, Confirmed Orders rows, PI Drafts rows, the Orders dashboard sections, the showroom editor's neighbours. Deleted: `src/hooks/useIntentWarm.ts` and the local `useWarmOnRest`. React Query data warm-ups (`prefetchQuery`) stay: they load data, not pages, and bring no stylesheet.
- **Cost, accepted by the owner.** The first click to a page waits for it (its loading skeleton shows) instead of being ready in advance.
- **Tests.** New guard `src/lib/navigation/noBackgroundPrefetch.test.ts`: AppLink is the only `next/link` importer, it forces prefetch off, nothing calls `router.prefetch`. Removed the superseded guards `intentPrefetch.test.ts`, `warmOnIntent.test.ts`, `notificationsIntentPrefetch.test.ts`; restated the pins in `orderStartupShape`, `taskNavigationPerformance`, `taskReturnPath`, `taskDetailOpenFromDrawer`, `controlCenterNav`, `finalApprovalScope` (one import line).
- **Supersedes.** PR #679 (For-you links on intent), closed unmerged.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR; no data involved.

## 2026-11-12 — Backup: the copy to the external drive now updates moved branches (PR #676, squash-merged 2026-11-12 10:20 IST as `26e8e2f1`) · no migration
- **Found.** The owner asked for a backup on the hard disk (D:) on 2026-11-12. After the copy, 20 of 2,398 refs in the drive's git mirror (`main` included) still named their old commit. The second-copy step skipped any file already on the drive with the same size, and every git ref is a 41-byte file, so a branch that moved was never updated. Objects were always copied, so no commit was missing, but a restore from the drive alone would have opened on old branch tips. The nightly task would have repeated it every night.
- **Fixed.** `copyGitMirror` (`scripts/backup/lib.mjs`) copies objects first (skipped when already there: they never change), then refs, HEAD and the rest always, so a ref never lands before its commit. `backup-all.mjs` uses it for `git/boe-task-management.git`; the other layers copy as before (storage stays incremental by size).
- **Proof.** Two new tests in `lib.test.mjs` (a moved ref with the same size reaches the drive; an object already there is left alone). The old rule, run on the same kind of fixture, leaves the stale ref. All 32 backup tests pass. Run on the real mirror against D:: 2,398 refs equal to local, no tip missing.
- **Installed.** 2026-11-12 10:20 IST, from the merged `main`: all 16 tools and 4 hooks in `BOE-Development\backups` are byte-equal to the repo, and the 32 backup tests pass from the installed folder. The old tools are kept in `BOE-Development\temporary\backup-tools-before-pr676`. The first nightly with the fix is 9:30 pm on 2026-11-12, and it copies to D: only if the drive is plugged in.
- **Rollback.** Revert the commit and reinstall the tools.

## 2026-11-11 — Sales: a WhatsApp chat is judged before it becomes a lead (PR #668, squash-merged 2026-11-11 20:56 IST as `20395a5f`) · `20270613000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-11 (about 19:30 IST, before the merge, alone; 20270610000000 stays pending on purpose)
- **Owner ask (2026-11-11).** "We get all our leads on the company's WhatsApp number" (+91 87691 13712); many chats are job seekers, suppliers or transport companies; the system should tell from the message whether it is a lead and only then move it to the Lead Master with a tag. Plan approved with its defaults.
- **Changed.** The WhatsApp route asks the database which numbers are already leads (`sales_chat_context`, service role), sends a NEW number's profile name and words (never the number) to Claude (`src/lib/salesLeads/classify.ts`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `WA_CLASSIFY_MODEL` optional), and passes the verdict to `capture_sales_chats` (replaced, same signature). Lead: as before, tagged `wa_tag` detected + `wa_reason`. Not a lead: kept in `sales_whatsapp_held` (last 5 messages, 30 days) and shown in Filtered chats (`/sales/lead-master/filtered-chats`, Sales `assign`) with This is a lead (`make_whatsapp_chat_a_lead`) and Clear (`clear_whatsapp_held_chat`). Unclear or any failure: a lead tagged Check, not allotted automatically, allotters told "WhatsApp chat to check"; its next messages are judged again (an ad on such a chat is kept). An ad chat is always a lead; a known lead is never judged. `sales_lead_row` re-issued with `wa_tag`, `wa_reason`.
- **Order is safe either way round, but migration first is the rule.** With the migration applied and the old code live, rows arrive without a verdict and behave exactly as before. With the new code live and the migration missing, `sales_chat_context` does not exist, the route answers 500 and Meta retries for 36 hours.
- **Not connected.** The owner's number +91 87691 13712 is still not on the WhatsApp API (coexistence provider undecided); this runs on Meta's test number until it is.
- **Rollback.** In the migration header: restore `capture_sales_chats` (20270609000000) and `sales_lead_row` (20270602000000), drop the four new functions and the table, drop the two columns. Leads created stay.
- **Apply (done 2026-11-11 by Claude at the owner's "apply the migration").** Read-only pre-check first: the live `capture_sales_chats` and `sales_lead_row` bodies matched 20270609000000 and 20270602000000 exactly (md5), none of the new objects existed, 20270612000000 applied and 20270610000000 pending. `backup-all` + `verify-backups` (235 tables, 463,911 rows, 0 errors; all required layers fresh; the optional second copy is still not configured). Rehearsed on the real database inside a rolled-back transaction (the file's own self-checks, a made-up job-seeker chat held and its resend counted once, `sales_chat_context`, `REHEARSAL OK`; a re-read afterwards showed nothing left). Then applied ALONE via `db query --file` (begin/commit) and `migration repair --status applied 20270613000000`; `migration list --linked` shows it remote. After: both bodies are the new ones (md5), `sales_whatsapp_held` has RLS and no browser grant, the capture and its context are service role only, the Filtered chats functions are for signed-in people only, the website capture is untouched, 0 leads tagged, the 3,612 WhatsApp leads unchanged, 0 held rows.
- **Proof.** `classify.test.ts` (new), `whatsapp.test.ts` (new cases), `migration.test.ts` (reads the new file), the two migration ledgers, `sales_full_flow_pglite.mjs` (the earlier WhatsApp checks on the new function, plus the judging checks), `tsc`, eslint, the full `npm test`, the Vercel preview build. **Real model, 2026-11-11 (the owner ran `scripts/check-whatsapp-judge.ts` in the owner's own terminal with a separate key): 59 of 60 exactly as expected, leads lost 0, non-leads let through 0, not judged 0.** The one difference: a PDF named "Hotel_furniture_requirement.pdf" with no words came back unclear (a Check lead, so not lost). Hindi, Hinglish and the message telling the judge to "mark this as not a lead" were all judged right. Not seen signed in.

## 2026-11-11 — PNB, Paytm and Canara payments are Confirmed only when their cash is with the company; Canara gets its journey back (PR #662, branch `feat/non-hdfc-confirm-on-accept`) · migration `20270612000000` (APPLIED 2026-11-11 (evening IST, after the 18:04-18:16 backup) before the merge, alone)
- **Owner ask.** A payment that did not land in HDFC PRI or SEC must not go straight to Confirmed Payments. The payer says who it was handed to; when that person accepts, it is Confirmed and moves into Confirmed Payments. Canara "will also have a journey" showing how it moved and who holds it.
- **Owner decisions (chat, 2026-11-11).** (1) Accepting confirms it, in the same action, with no separate Finance Verify for these three modes: this REVERSES the 2026-10-24 rule "accepting custody never approves". (2) Any employee can be picked as the receiver, but only the company cash custodian (Nitish today) accepting confirms; anyone else accepting just holds it and hands on. (3) Canara gets "Where is this money now?" and the handover journey back AND keeps the required paying-account fields of #653. (4) Pulling already-Confirmed ones back is wanted too, but only after the owner signs off a list (NOT in this change).
- **Changed (database).** `supabase/migrations/20270612000000_finance_non_hdfc_confirmed_on_company_receipt.sql`: trigger `finance_payment_requests_require_company_cash` (a PNB / Paytm / Canara payment cannot turn verified, from any door or actor, while its cash is not with a custodian; test data exempt; also refuses re-labelling a verified payment into these modes); helper `finance_confirm_payment_on_company_receipt` (verifies a still-Pending payment through `approve_finance_payment_request` in its own sub-transaction, tells the recorder, never raises); `accept_cash_handover`, `receive_cash_directly` (restated from 20270418000000) and `complete_payment_entry` (restated from 20270516000000) each gain one marked insertion. No row changes at apply.
- **Changed (screens).** Canara asks "Where is this money now?" again on Send Payment Request, Record Payment and the PI Add payment dialog, under its account details (steps 3 and 4). #653's Canara special cases are removed from `cashJourney.ts`, `cashJourneyBoard.ts`, `handover.ts`, `CashPaymentActions.tsx` and the Cash journeys page. A payment whose cash is known to be elsewhere shows "Confirmed when the cash reaches the company" instead of Verify / Approve (Payment Requests popup via `awaitsCash`, PI and Order payment rows via `ApproveUnlessCashPending`); database refusals read as sentences through `bankVerifyRefusal`; the three accept dialogs say a custodian's acceptance also confirms.
- **Not changed.** HDFC PRI / SEC (verified only against the bank), the payment lock, "Not received" (final), the Order cash exception (it can no longer arise for new payments), permissions, notification types.
- **Known gaps.** The Order team is not sent the "payment landed" notice when a payment is confirmed by acceptance (the recorder is). A custodian without finance.approve receives the cash but the payment stays Pending for an approver (WARNING only, no screen text). The pull-back of already-Confirmed payments is not built. Not seen on screen.
- **Proof.** `supabase/tests/non_hdfc_confirmed_on_company_receipt_pglite.mjs`: 18 of 18 on in-memory Postgres with the REAL migrations 20270410 → 20270418 → 20270612 (the migration's own apply-time assertions run), including the negative control that Finance and an admin are refused before the cash is in. `src/lib/finance/nonHdfcConfirmedOnCompanyReceipt.test.ts` pins the restated bodies against their sources (only additions) and the screens.
- **Apply / rollout (done 2026-11-11 at the owner's yes).** backup-all + verify-backups first (235 tables, 463,891 rows; all required layers fresh; the optional second copy is still not configured). The file was rehearsed on the real database inside a rolled-back transaction (marker row correct, a re-read after the rollback showed nothing left), then applied ALONE via `db query --file` (begin/commit) + `migration repair --status applied`; `migration list --linked` shows it remote. `20270610000000` (#652) is STILL PENDING on purpose. After the apply: the trigger is live, the helpers are not callable by clients, anon cannot call accept, the 43 payment rows are unchanged, and ONE verified PNB / Paytm / Canara payment has its cash elsewhere (it stays Confirmed: the pull-back candidate). Rollback: drop the trigger and re-emit the three bodies from their sources (the helpers can stay).

## 2026-11-11 — SEO: the discussion brief drawn as a case for a decision about the role (PR #661, c9f1ce32) · no migration
- **Owner request.** Rebuild the Admin-only discussion brief so every fact, chart and claim can be discussed with the SEO person and answered with data, with research on three claims (a Google update in September 2026, content not updated, no guest posting) and a kind way to hold the conversation.
- **Changed.** A brief that has `claims` is drawn by `SeoBriefCase.tsx`: headline numbers, two monthly charts with Google's confirmed updates marked, rankings by quarter as a coloured table, the flow from clicks to leads, our base against the sites that beat us, the rivals that rose, each claim with our data, Google's own statements and a line to say, likely counters, what was missed, credit and fairness, running SEO without the role, and the conversation in steps. An older brief draws as before.
- **Data check.** Sections may be left out whole, a quarter cell may be blank, and the review's scored, others, yours and next are optional. The September brief still passes.
- **Order.** Merge first, then send the new brief: until this is live the door would refuse a brief without the scored section.
- **Proof.** New tests for the case layout, the quarter colours, the chart axis and the data check. The real document checked by the door's own check and drawn with the app's stylesheets at desktop and phone. Not seen signed in.

## 2026-11-11 — Canara payments: the paying account instead of "Where is this money now?" (PR #653, squash-merged 2026-11-11 14:38 IST as `06167f61`; its "no handover" half was reversed the same day, see the entry above) · no migration
- **Owner ask.** A Canara payment is money that landed in a bank account, so there is no handover journey. The person adding it (a salesperson, a manager or the owner) gives the account it came from; Nitish (the company cash custodian) then accepts it, meaning the money reached the company. Decisions in chat: the three fields describe the paying account and Bank is any bank (Canara is only BOE's account name); account holder and account number are required, bank, payment details and the screenshot are optional; Nitish's Accept means "reached the company" only (Finance still verifies, rule 3c of the Finance dossier); stored in the existing note, no SQL.
- **Changed.** Send Payment Request, Record Payment and the PI "Add payment" dialog no longer ask "Where is this money now?" for Canara (PNB and Paytm still do; `modeAsksWhereMoneyIs`); they ask for **Account holder name\***, **Account number\*** and **Bank** (`CanaraAccountFields`), and open the proof / reference / screenshot section. The three answers are saved as labelled lines at the top of the note to Finance (`composeCanaraNote`, `src/lib/finance/canaraAccount.ts`), so the payment popup shows them wherever it shows the note. Edit Payment Request no longer shows the "use Hand over cash" line for Canara.
- **After saving.** A Canara payment reads "Waiting for the company to accept" (not "With <recorder>"); no screen offers a Hand over for it (Cash journeys, the unclosed-money card, the modules attention panel, the Hand over cash picker); Nitish's Receive button is called **Accept** with a Canara sentence. The payment stays tracked, so an Order's payments check still lists it until he accepts.
- **Not changed.** The database: `payment_mode_requires_custody` still includes canara, `create_cash_handover` still allows a handover of one (no screen offers it), `receive_cash_directly` is the Accept, nothing is enforced server-side for the two required fields (a form rule). Payments already in the old Canara flow keep their history and open handovers.
- **Proof.** `canaraAccount.test.ts` (20 new), three pin tests restated (`paymentFormsRecordEdit`, `paymentRequestForm`, `custodyTrail`), the Finance and Orders suites (8,759 tests) pass, `tsc` and eslint clean. NOT seen on screen or signed in.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR; nothing in the database was touched. Payments saved meanwhile keep their account lines in the note.

## 2026-11-11 — Tasks: automatic escalation removed (PR #652, squash-merged 2026-11-11 14:22 IST as `8ff4dafd`) · migration `20270610000000` → APPLIED 2026-11-12 13:00 IST (see the 2026-11-12 entry)
- **Owner ask.** Remove the escalation feature: "not solving any purpose and just sends comments and messages on repeat after certain hours".
- **What it was.** The hourly pg_cron job `run_task_health_check()` added two lines to a task's Activity, written under the task creator's name: "Auto-escalated: overdue with no action for 24 hours" and "Auto-escalated: no update for 72 hours" (action `escalated`, once per task each). It sent no notification since `20261015000000`.
- **Changed.** `20270610000000_task_health_check_stops_escalating.sql` replaces the function without those two writes. The stale flag (`is_stale`, `stale_day_count`, the `stale_flagged` line) is carried over as identical text, and the overdue-and-silent skip stays so the same tasks are flagged as before. `isHiddenActivity` (`src/lib/ui.ts`) is applied in `buildActivityLog` (`taskDetailLoad.ts`), so Task Detail's Activity no longer draws old `escalated` lines; the two dead label branches in `src/app/tasks/[id]/page.tsx` are gone. Nothing is deleted from `task_activity_log`.
- **Scoring.** `escalated` stays in `MEANINGFUL_ACTIONS` (`teamPerformance.ts`), so rows already written still count as the creator's active day; new ones stop. Hiding the lines in the page does not change any score. Net effect: an overdue task no longer hands its creator a free active day.
- **Not changed (owner has not asked).** Dashboard `EscalationListDrawer`, Manager "Silent 72h+", `escalationLevel()`, `TaskCard showEscalation`; the stale flag; the cron schedule (the entry is not in any migration); `TaskDetailPanel.tsx` (the merged drawer redesign #654 already skips `escalated` lines through its own list); `/api/task-detail` (no caller).
- **Proof.** `escalationRemovalMigration.test.ts` (14) fails if anything is added, if more than the escalation writes and the 72-hour wrapper are removed, or if the stale block differs by a character (checked by breaking the file on purpose). A rehearsal on a throwaway in-memory Postgres (PGlite) with ten test tasks: the old function wrote the 24h line for 2 tasks and the 72h line for 4 and flagged 2 stale; the new one wrote the same stale flags and lines and no `escalated` line; a second run changed nothing; an older `escalated` line stayed; re-running the old text restored the old behaviour exactly. **NOT done:** the cron entry, the live function text and the number of existing `escalated` rows were NOT read, and a rollback rehearsal on the real database was NOT run — production reads were refused to me (permission classifier). The stand-in schema is not production.
- **Owner's steps.** Run the one read-only check in the PR (cron entry, live function matches `20261015000000`, count of old lines), `supabase db push --dry-run` (only `20270610000000` pending; `--include-all` trap), apply, then the post-apply check in the PR. Backup before applying (Master Instruction Sheet section 9).
- **Rollback.** Run the `CREATE OR REPLACE` statement from `20261015000000_task_health_check_stops_notifying.sql`; the next hourly run resumes the two lines. Code: revert the PR (hidden lines reappear).

## 2026-11-08 — Minop feed: the pull can no longer skip a punch the nightly sync repairs (branch `fix/minop-feed-pull-no-gap`, PR open, NOT merged) · no migration
- **Found while answering the owner's "no data missed in any case".** The pull's cursor followed `first_seen_at`. The nightly sync (`syncMachineAttendance`) adds a punch the webhook failed to record with its OLD receipt time, so an ERP whose cursor had already passed that time never pulled it. Only a punch whose push ALSO failed could be lost, but the promise is no loss in any case.
- **Changed.** The pull now runs in insert order (`created_at`, then `id`), and the cursor carries `created_at`. It lists a punch only once it has been in the table 2 minutes (`PARTNER_SETTLE_MS`), so a write still finishing cannot land behind a cursor just handed out. The push brings fresh punches within seconds, so nothing waits on that. The reply shape, the push and every other part are unchanged. An older cursor still decodes and at worst re-sends punches, which the ERP skips by `id`.
- **Proof.** All 247 Minop tests pass, including a repaired punch with an old receipt time that still comes after the cursor, the settle bound, and the exact query (`created_at=lt.<now - 2 min>`, `order=created_at.asc,id.asc`, the cursor `or` filter on `created_at`). Type check and lint are clean. No index: the table grows by a few thousand rows a year.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR.

## 2026-11-07 — Payment Requests: merge a duplicate Sales request into the payment already confirmed (PR #639 squash-merged as 4981893b, 10:21 UTC, on the owner's word; Production deployment 7619898821 success 10:23 UTC) · 20270608000000 → APPLIED 2026-11-07 by the owner, before the merge (the screen calls the new door)
- **Owner request.** A payment is often recorded by the owner and then sent again by Sales (the screenshot: P-AA-0093, ₹13,38,565.00, already the verified Suspense payment P-AA-0089, linked to its credit). The window must say plainly that it is a request, compare it with the confirmed payment, and let the owner merge it: not rejected, nothing for Sales to do, the old record stays and its activity shows who sent the request and that it was merged as a duplicate. Plan, a mockup (Design canvas, five boards), then "Yes, build it" with all four recommended choices: find the earlier payment by its details as well as by the credit it holds; apply Sales' destination to the old payment; the request leaves Sales' list with a notice; HDFC PRI / SEC only.
- **Changed (screens).** In the payment window of a pending HDFC request the bank panel opens with "Sent by <name>", a note that this may already be in Confirmed Payments, and the request beside the confirmed payment (Added by, Amount, Payment date, Account, Bank credit, UTR, Client, For, Proof, a verdict on each) with **Merge into P-AA-xxxx**. Merge asks first and lists what will happen (optional note). When a merge is not allowed the window says why in words and draws no button. The old "unlink, delete, then verify" advice stays only as the fallback when the look-alike cannot be read. New `src/app/finance/bankDuplicate.tsx` and `src/lib/finance/paymentDuplicates.ts`; small edits to `bankMarkUi.tsx`, `page.tsx`, `paymentRequestsDetails.tsx` (the notice), `paymentActivityLabels.ts` + `PaymentRequestActivity.tsx` (two events), `notify.ts` and `/api/finance/notify` (`finance_merged`, sent as the existing `finance_linked` enum value, so no enum change), `globals.css` (new `.bank-dup*` block; stacks on a phone).
- **Changed (database).** `20270608000000_finance_merge_duplicate_payment_request.sql`: `merge_payment_request(request, into, note)`, definer, fixed `search_path`, no anon; needs `finance.reconcile` and `finance.approve`, never on your own request unless you may verify your own. Needs a pending request and a VERIFIED payment of the same HDFC account and the EXACT amount; refused when the old payment is already allocated somewhere else. One transaction: the request's intent (or PI-page allocation) is cancelled / reversed and placed on the old payment through the canonical allocators; its bank links are released and its proof files move; the request is soft-removed with the `delete_own_payment_request` mechanism (not rejected, no new status); `request_merged` and `request_merged_in` are written. The activity `event_type` check is restated with 16 values.
- **Not changed.** No status, permission, Order or PI total, payment ID or amount. A merged request keeps its ids and history; nothing is deleted.
- **Proof.** `supabase/tests/payment_request_merge_pglite.mjs`: 30 checks on the real allocators, freeze trigger and bank unlink, and 15 deliberate breaks of the migration all caught (`MUTATE=<name>`); `paymentDuplicates.test.ts` (23), `bankDuplicate.dom.test.tsx` (17), activity label tests, 18 deliberate breaks of the code all caught; both migration-ledger lists in `participantAndOrderTotalSecurity.test.ts` name the migration. Final full suite on the rebased tree: 20,235 tests, 20,226 passed, 0 failed, 9 skipped. Type check, whole-project lint and `next build` clean. The rehearsal file also ran on PGlite, with and without a seeded P-AA-0093 / P-AA-0089 pair, and on the REAL database in a rolled-back transaction (the owner ran it: `REHEARSAL_OK`, including a real merge of P-AA-0093 into P-AA-0089 as an admin; both rows read back untouched). Not covered: a merge by a non-admin verifier on the real database; not seen signed in or on a phone.
- **Steps taken, in this order.** Backup-all by hand (the one case it is still run by hand; database export 20261107T085716Z, 234 tables, 462,955 rows); the rolled-back rehearsal `supabase db query --linked --file <rehearsal.sql>` (`REHEARSAL_OK`); `db push --dry-run` (only 20270608000000 pending); the owner applied it with `supabase db push` from the worktree and `supabase migration list --linked` showed it in both columns; merge on the owner's word. No backup after the merge (owner decision: the 9:30 pm task covers the day). Rollback: drop the function and restore the 14-value event check (header of the file); a merge already done stays as history.

## 2026-11-06 — BOE Credits: a review month that is still open follows the saved monthly target (PR #627 squash-merged as ad44cccd by the owner 2026-11-06 05:04Z, deployed 05:06Z) · 20270605000000 → APPLIED 2026-11-06 about 05:12Z on the owner's yes (the migration list shows it in both columns)
- **Owner report.** The admin lowered "Minimum Approved Reviews Per Month" from 3 to 1 on `/payroll/credits`. October's Month close table still read "2 of 3" and "1 of 3", "Below target", and closing October would have lapsed those credits. Cause: each month row copied the target in force when its first review was approved (`minimum_reviews_snapshot`), the guard trigger refused any later change, and the recount qualified only against the copy, so a change to the saved target never reached a month that already had a row. Plan agreed ("Yes, build it"); the owner's intent: each month's qualified-or-not status follows the target they saved.
- **Changed (database).** `20270605000000_boe_credits_open_months_follow_target.sql`: the months guard lets the minimum move only while the month is open and not finalized; `refresh_boe_credit_review_month()` reads the newest saved target for such a month and qualifies against it (closing a month recounts first, so it is judged against the target in force when closed); new `refresh_open_boe_credit_review_months()`; new trigger `boe_credit_settings_refresh_open_months` (AFTER INSERT on `boe_credit_settings`), so saving a target on the BOE Credits page applies it at once; and a one-time recount at the end of the file. Qualified months stay qualified when the target is raised; closed and lapsed months keep the minimum they were closed under and are not reopened. No ledger row is written.
- **THE MONEY STEP.** The one-time recount qualifies every open month that already meets the saved target and so turns its pending credits into spendable ones. With the target at 1 and October as it stood on 2026-11-05 that is two employees (2 reviews = 2 credits, 1 review = 1 credit, about Rs 150). The file's own checks run in the same transaction and abort the apply if the result is not exactly the rule.
- **Changed (screens, wording only).** Month close: "1 verified review" (singular), plus "A month still open follows the target saved now"; the settings form hint and footer say the monthly minimum reaches open months and that qualified or closed months keep theirs; the employee guide line says the same. "x of N" is the month row's own minimum, so it follows the database.
- **Rollout, as it actually went.** Planned: backup, apply, then merge. The owner merged #627 first (05:04:53Z, deployed 05:06:42Z) while the backup was still finishing; harmless, because the merged code is wording only, but for about ten minutes the screens said open months follow the target while the database did not. Backup `database\20261106T045007Z` (234 tables, 462,536 rows, pg_dump OK for the 3 restricted finance tables, `verify-backups` all required layers fresh; the second copy failed because D: was not plugged in). Then, on "Yes, apply it": the file in the worktree was byte-identical to merged main (sha-256 58f317be…); a read-only preview of production showed the saved target at 1 and exactly two open months that would qualify (October, 3 reviews, 3.00 credits), plus one September month with 0 reviews that would stay open; `supabase db push --dry-run` listed only `20270605000000`; `db push` applied it (exit 0). Rollback in the migration header restores behaviour only; a qualified month stays qualified by design.
- **Result read back from production after the apply (no names).** October: 2 months now `qualified` under minimum 1 (3 reviews, 3 credits), 1 month still `qualified` under 3 (4 reviews, 5 credits, untouched). September: 2 months `qualified` under 3 (7 reviews, 7 credits, untouched), 1 month `open` with 0 reviews now carrying minimum 1. Total provisional credits 0.00 (it was 3.00). The settings trigger exists; `anon` and `authenticated` cannot execute the recount. The credit balance view was not used to total spendable credits: it is read under a login with no `auth.uid()`, so that figure is not trusted. Not seen signed in by the owner: Month close should now read "2 of 1" and "1 of 1", Qualified.
- **Proof.** `supabase/tests/boe_credits_open_months_target_pglite.mjs` (real chain on PGlite, 29 checks: reproduces the reported defect before the file, applies it twice, lower / raise / approve / reverse / close / guard / privileges, and four deliberately broken copies each stopped by the file's own check), `openMonthsTarget.test.ts`, the two migration ledgers, type check, lint, full suite (see the PR). Not seen signed in; production data was not read (reads refused), so the real October months were not rehearsed, only the same numbers on a replayed schema.

## 2026-11-05 — Payroll: the payslip lists the month's review credits (PR #625 squash-merged as aa1f4b46, 2026-11-05 19:04Z, deployed 19:07Z) · no SQL
- **Owner request.** Jasvi's October payslip showed one "BOE Credit Addition" of ₹75 (1.5 credits) although she had four approved reviews that month, each with its own credit. Cause: a review's credit is only EARNED on the ledger; it reaches salary when the employee applies it from their own payslip, and the payslip showed only the applied amount. Plan agreed ("Yes, build it"): show the breakdown, do not change how credits become salary.
- **Changed.** The payslip detail payload (admin and employee) gains `review_credits`; a new card "BOE Credits · October reviews" sits under Salary Calculation and Adjustments: one row per approved review (day, Text or Image review, reference, credit; a reversed credit is struck through and not counted), then Earned from the month's reviews (with the rupee value at the current rate), Applied to this salary, Spendable now (across all months), the month's target sentence (pending / reached / lapsed) and, for the admin only, that credits reach salary only when the employee applies them. No card for an employee with no review credit that month. New `src/lib/payroll/reviewCredits.ts` (pure), `reviewCreditsStore.ts` (the read) and `src/components/payroll/ReviewCreditsCard.tsx`; `PayrollDetailView` and `resultDetailPayload.ts` pass it through. "Applied" is read from the settlement and the live balance from the employee page's own figure, because that page refreshes those after Apply / Change / Remove and would otherwise show the card stale until a reload. A read error drops the card and logs; the payslip still opens.
- **Not changed.** No salary figure, no settlement arithmetic, no engine input. Auto-applying a month's credits (so nobody has to press Apply) was NOT built: it changes the rule decided in Phase 1D and moves money for every employee, so it waits for an owner decision.
- **Database.** None (reads five existing tables). Rollback: revert the PR.
- **Proof.** `reviewCredits.test.ts`, `resultDetailPayload.reviewCredits.test.ts`, `ReviewCreditsCard.test.tsx` (see the dossier); full suite 20,036 tests, 0 failed; type check, lint and build clean. Not seen signed in, on a phone, or against Jasvi's real October data (production reads were refused to the agent).
- **Merge.** On the owner's yes ("Yes, merge it"), after they thought they had merged it: GitHub still showed #625 open with every check green, so the agent squash-merged it with `gh pr merge --squash`, pinned to head `5262a15e` and run only while the state read `OPEN CLEAN`. Production deployment `7582756337` (GitHub deployments API) succeeded at 19:07:06Z, about 2 minutes after the merge. The live bundle was not grepped. No backup was run after the merge (owner decision: the 9:30 pm task covers the day).

## 2026-11-05 — Payroll: a payslip can hold half a paid leave (PR #619 squash-merged as b1588211, 2026-11-05 14:42Z, deployed 14:44Z) · 20270604000000 → APPLIED 2026-11-05 on the owner's yes (the migration list shows it, both columns read back as numeric(3,1), all 39 payslip rows kept with values 0 or 1)
- **Owner report.** One employee's salary was missing from the October 2026 payroll after generating it. Found: the October run saved 8 of 11 payslips; two employees (15 and 14 days present) crashed on save. The rules award 0.5 paid leave for 11-15 days present, and `payroll_results.paid_leave_available` / `paid_leave_used` were `smallint` (Postgres `22P02` on 0.5). No earlier month had anyone in that band. Each attendance correction regenerates the payslip too, so those two could not have been corrected one day at a time through the band either.
- **Changed.** Nothing in the app. The two columns become `numeric(3,1)`; existing 0 and 1 are kept; the file ends with a check that aborts the apply if either column is not `numeric(3,1)`.
- **Database.** `20270604000000_payroll_results_half_paid_leave.sql`. Rollback in its header (safe only before a half has been stored). Rehearsed in PGlite, 8 checks, including the exact October error before the apply and the apply twice; the linked-database rehearsal is refused by the permission check, so none was run on production.
- **Proof.** `halfPaidLeaveColumns.test.ts` (fails without the migration), `supabase/tests/payroll_half_paid_leave_pglite.mjs`, full suite, type check, lint (see the PR).
- **Then, the same day.** The backup (database snapshot `20261105T115956Z`, complete) and the apply came first. After the apply the October exhibition-day corrections went in: 4 by the owner for one person by hand, 38 by script through the same service the Payroll screen uses, after a read-only dry run; each saved payslip read back equal to the dry run. Generate was not needed, because the first correction created each missing payslip. Still open for the owner: the 13th, 2 Oct as a holiday, the other absences, and locking October.
- **Merge.** On the owner's yes. Auto-merge is not allowed in this repository, so it was merged by hand once `verify` passed on the latest commit. Main moved twice while the PR was open (docs conflicts only: changelog and inventory). The production deployment of the merge commit succeeded at 14:44Z; there is no application code in the PR.

## 2026-11-05 — Backups: only one backup-all at a time (lock file) · no SQL
- **Why.** On 2026-11-05 a hand run and a second `backup-all` (older tools) overlapped; the second failed on 6 tables but finished 1 s later and overwrote `status/database.json` with ok:false, so `verify-backups` said FAIL right after a good backup.
- **Changed (`scripts/backup`, no app code).** New `lock.mjs`; `backup-all.mjs` takes `status/backup-all.lock` first and releases it on any exit. A live holder makes the second run exit 2 with who and since when, touching nothing. A dead or over-6-hours-old holder is replaced. New `lock.test.mjs` (stale, live, released after success / throw / exit 1, status files byte-identical when refused). Quick hook runs deliberately not locked (details in `03_BACKUP_AND_RECOVERY.md`).
- **Database.** None. Rollback: revert the PR. **Apply step:** run `node scripts/backup/install-backup-system.mjs` from the main checkout after the merge (owner's yes first).

## 2026-11-05 — Backups: finish under the Terminal panel's git setting, read a dropped page again, start Docker themselves (PR #621) · no SQL
- **Owner request.** Make `backup-all.mjs` end `{"code":true,"database":true}` every time, and find why the 9:30 pm task had no complete backup for 2 days. Three faults came from the hand run of 2026-11-05.
- **Why the nightly "failed".** The task ran; its database step did not. 3 Nov 21:30: `order_submission_fabric_finish_lines: terminated`. 4 Nov 21:30: laptop off, Task Scheduler's catch-up ran at 09:06 on 5 Nov: `sales_lead_activities: terminated`. A run that does not finish cleanly writes no `covered.json`, so the "no complete backup" reminder kept firing. Task Scheduler said 0 every time because the `.cmd` returned the reminder pop-up's exit code. The 2 Nov run never finished (no end line in the log, cause not recorded). Sleep was ruled out: no standby event inside any failing window.
- **Cause of "terminated".** Not one table and not Supabase: the laptop's own network dropping mid-page (`ECONNRESET`, and once DNS `ENOTFOUND` for about 15 s). It hit `notifications`, `sales_leads`, `sales_lead_activities`, `sales_lead_events`, `sales_lead_import_refs` and others, a different one each time. The old code retried only `fetch()`; the body was read afterwards by `res.json()`, outside any retry, so one cut page lost the whole table.
- **Changed (`scripts/backup`, no app code).** `lib.mjs`: `mirrorGit` / `mirrorGitTry` (`git --git-dir`), `describeError`, `readWithShrink`, `waitForDocker`, `restrictedTablesLayer`. `backup-code.mjs` and `verify-backups.mjs` address the mirror with `--git-dir`, because the Terminal panel forces `safe.bareRepository=explicit` ("fatal: not in a git directory"). `backup-database.mjs` reads each page (request and body) inside a retry at 1000, 1000, 250, 250, 100, 100 rows with a 120 s limit per try and 5, 10, 15, 20, 25 s waits, logs the real cause on a `RETRY` line, records `retried` in `MANIFEST.json`, prints `!!! TABLES NOT EXPORTED` for any table that still fails, and starts Docker Desktop (waits up to 4 min) before pg_dump. `verify-backups.mjs`: new layer "finance tables the service role cannot read" (FAIL when pg_dump did not cover them). `boe-backup-daily.cmd`: start and finish lines with both exit codes, and it exits with the backup's then verify's code. Details in `03_BACKUP_AND_RECOVERY.md`; the garbled schedule line there (eaten backslashes) is repaired.
- **Database.** None. Rollback: revert the PR, then run `install-backup-system.mjs`. **Apply step:** the nightly task runs the copy in `backups\tools`, so after the merge run `node scripts/backup/install-backup-system.mjs` from the main checkout.
- **Proof.** 21 tests in `scripts/backup` (new `reliability.test.mjs`, `backup-database.test.mjs`): `backup-code.mjs --quick` under forced `explicit`, the real database script against a fake REST API that cuts responses off (fails on the old script with `gadgets: terminated`, the production message), Docker start/wait, the layer, and the `.cmd` exit codes; eslint clean. Hand runs of `backup-all.mjs`, both `{"code":true,"database":true}`: Terminal panel under `explicit` (233 tables, 462,274 rows, pg_dump OK; 4 tables needed pages read again) and Bash tool (233 tables, 462,285 rows, 0 errors, pg_dump OK); `verify-backups.mjs` passes every layer except the second copy (D: not plugged in). The Terminal run used a 1.5 s wait between tries (made 5 s afterwards, because a 15 s network outage only just fit); the Bash run used the final code and needed no retry. Not tested: the Docker start itself (Docker was already running); the nightly task with the fix (not installed yet). Two backups overlapped that afternoon and the older tools' failed one overwrote `status/database.json` last: a lock against that is a follow-up.

## 2026-11-03 — Sales: website leads allotted by themselves (PR #608) · 20270602000000 → APPLIED 2026-11-03, before the merge (seen in the migration list)

A website lead is given at once in working hours: Premium tries Senior, Mid, Junior, Backup; any other lead goes to Senior, Mid and Junior alike (Backup on overflow), to whoever is punched in, under their limit, with the fewest fresh leads. A lead nobody can take, or after hours, waits and the morning run gives it; Allot waiting leads now on the Lead Master. A Premium tag with its reason on cards and rows, a Big quantity tag, "Allotted to X automatically: …" in the history, and a Premium-with-a-Junior list on the Team page.

Priority is a coloured dot with a neutral word; Last update is muted; Cancel is grey until hovered. Search and both filters use the shared `.boe-input` (one look, with aria-labels, 44px and 16px on a phone). Team response shows no strip for "Too early" people. Phone cards keep their words and gain the same two icons. Breakpoints are 1120 / 960 / 860px of list width.

## 2026-11-03 — Orders: the Record dispatch screen is scan first (PR #600 merged as e980e2da, 2026-11-03) · no migration

Choose file, drag a file onto the box, or take a photo on a phone; several papers per box; Remove. A paper in the wrong box moves to the right one. The paper being checked shows beside the details. The save bar says whether Save closes the Order as Dispatched or leaves it where it is.

## 2026-11-03 — Orders: dispatch drives the Order's status; Confirmed Orders opens on Running (PR #598 merged as 5b37b88f, deployed 2026-11-03 09:04 UTC) · 20270528000000 → APPLIED 2026-11-03
- **Owner request.** A fully or partly dispatched Order must show in Factory Status. Fully dispatched = Dispatched and not in the Running list; the list opens on Running with All orders one click away; part sent with the rest being made stays Running; part sent with the rest fully ready is Ready for Dispatch. Plan and mockups approved 2026-11-03.
- **Changed.** The last piece recorded closes the Order as Dispatched (and removing a dispatch reopens an automatic close); Ready for Dispatch can go back to Running; an Order with anything dispatched cannot be cancelled (revise the PI down to what was sent). Factory Status says Not Aligned / Aligned, On hold, Ready for dispatch or Dispatched, with "Part dispatched · 22 of 74 pcs" under it. A search or the operations queue looks at every status.
- **Merged with the spacing change (#597).** That PR made every column but Project code and Due a percentage; its Factory Status share left about 100px for a line that needs up to 187px. Measured with the real font loaded (`document.fonts.ready`): Factory Status is 21% (260px at the floor, 56px of it the air before it), the floor is 1240px (was 1120), and #597's other percentages were scaled by 1120/1240 so each keeps the pixel width it measured; Client 177px, Due 122px. A longer line (a four-digit order) wraps instead of spilling.
- **Database.** `order_dispatch_pieces`, `order_dispatch_sync_status`, two deferred constraint triggers (`order_dispatch_lots`, `order_pi_versions`), `orders_enforce_status_transition` re-emitted, `orders_dispatch_progress()` for the list. No existing Order is touched: the backfill is a query for the owner (foot of the migration).
- **Rollout.** Apply 20270528000000 first (a backup, then `supabase db push --dry-run`, from an up-to-date checkout); the list works before it, with no dispatch line. The owner's rollback rehearsal is `~/BOE-Development/temporary/dispatch-status-rehearsal.sql` (prints `REHEARSAL OK` and how many Orders the backfill would close). Rollback is in the migration header.
- **Proof.** The real Payment plan, Dispatch and roll-up migrations on PGlite over stubs (`supabase/tests/order_dispatch_status_pglite.mjs`, 32 checks, two mutations caught), the rehearsal checks on PGlite, `tsc`, the whole suite, lint. Rehearsed on the real database in a rolled-back transaction (`REHEARSAL OK`, nothing left behind), backed up first (database snapshot 20261103T084534Z), then applied 2026-11-03 by one `db push`; `migration list --linked` shows both versions on both sides. **Not yet seen signed in.** Order 0536 was the one Order with a dispatch and every piece was sent, so on the owner's word ("Close Order 536") it was closed as Dispatched the same day by the backfill statement, limited to that Order and checking its own result before it committed: Running -> Dispatched, dispatched on 2026-10-27, one status_changed row with source dispatch, no other Order touched.
- **Not done.** The scan-first Record dispatch screen (second PR, no SQL); a notification for an automatic close; a payment gate.

At the 1120px floor nothing is newly cut: every name fits except the worst-case "IBC Hospitality Private Limited", which was cut before too. The width test now pins the nine widths, Client at 174px or more and Due at 102px or more at the floor. Not seen signed in by the builder.

## 2026-11-03 — Sales: the ten review fixes, and ClickUp's full history (PR #596 merged as 594daf93) · 20270527000000 → APPLIED 2026-11-03 (in the same push as 20270528000000; it had been merged first, so the Sales allot lists, Team people and Import failed until then)
- **Owner request.** Check the whole flow as a salesperson, a manager and Admin; fix all ten problems found; make sure the ClickUp upload loses no conversation and keeps its exact dates; show a test upload first.
- **Fixed.** A lead can be given only to somebody with a Sales job (it was the Orders sales-candidate rule). Converted asks for contact, requirement, quantity and value. Only a step past the furthest stage reached is checked, so Undo and leaving Follow up later never ask again, and Undo no longer says "Undone" when nothing moved. Not interested is logged as a call. Imported leads nobody tried wait for their day in My day and At risk. A repeat enquiry is marked and goes to the top even after earlier work. The lead on top of My day stays put through refreshes. Lead Master history shows moves, enquiries and imports; history names every changed fact in words. The evening alert reaches managers who hold leads. The Team filter keeps everybody listed. Alerts run from `/api/sales/alerts/morning` and `/evening` (no query string in the cron path).
- **ClickUp's full history.** The CSV export carries 10,497 of the 87,336 comments, undated. `scripts/clickup-history.mjs` reads them all from ClickUp's API with the owner's own token (`--sample 5` first); the Import page takes its `history.jsonl` with the CSVs and gives each comment its real date and author, each ClickUp status its start date, and moves the lead's contact dates to ClickUp's. Twice adds nothing.
- **Rollout.** Apply 20270527000000 before merging: the screens call `list_sales_lead_holders`. Then give everybody who sells a Sales job, or nobody can be given a lead.
- **Proof.** The whole journey as every role on the real migrations in PGlite (`supabase/tests/sales_full_flow_pglite.mjs`, 50 checks); the real exports again: 10,960 leads and 332 notes, My day on day one 20 for the two people who would have had 56 and 40. **Not run against production or seen signed in; the history download needs the owner's token and has not run against ClickUp.**

## 2026-11-03 — Sales, going live: ClickUp import, Team page, Sales team tab, alerts (PR #595, 972413c3) · 20270526000000 and 20270526000100 → APPLIED 2026-11-03, before the merge
- **Owner request.** Make Sales live today: the ClickUp subscription has ended and will not be renewed. Build every remaining page without waiting for approval, and allow all the leads to be imported so they can be easily managed.

Control Center → Access → Sales team sets each person's job and a senior's juniors. `/sales/notifications` is the Sales inbox, with a Sales category for phone alerts.
- **Alerts.** A new lead, a repeat enquiry and a manager's Remind tell the holder at once. Twice a day (`/api/sales/alerts`, two new Vercel crons): follow-ups in the morning, first calls missed in the evening, then manager level the next morning.
- **Owner decisions used.** First call the same working day; salesperson then manager level; value required from Negotiation; red limits unchanged.
- **Mapping chosen without asking, as instructed.** Important Bulk is Under process, Warm; plain Bulk is Under process, Cold; marked urgent or important is Hot; Overseas is Under process; everything in an "All Dead Leads" list is Dead, including "live again"; "No requirement" and "Not relevant" join the dead reasons.
- **Rollout.** Apply both migrations before merging, in order: the code names the new notification types, and an unknown type fails the whole feed. Then give people their jobs, then import.

Screens seen with made-up data on desktop and phone. **Not run against production or seen signed in.**

## 2026-11-02 — Sales, Lead Master (PR #593, dfe7a3c3) · 20270524000000 → APPLIED
- **Owner request.** A Lead Master section in Sales: one place to store every lead and record to whom it has been allotted, replacing the Google Sheet "Leads Master - 2026". The contact number is held in one format and is unique; entering an existing number shows the lead already added, who holds it and the day it came in (the link to that lead is dead until a Lead Management module exists). The date is filled in automatically and stands for the day the lead was received. The layout was approved from a mock-up; the owner then **removed allocation from Add lead**: a lead is saved not allotted, and is allotted afterwards from a dropdown of sales candidates on each list row, with a confirmation only when an existing name is **changed** (decided: the first allotment does not ask).
- **Added.** `/sales` redirects to `/sales/lead-master` (period cards Today / Last 7 days / Last 30 days / All time, search, source filter, "Allotted to" chips with a "Not allotted" queue, the table or cards on a phone, a lead panel with history and edit) and `/sales/lead-master/new` (number, source, optional details; "Save and add another"). `SalesLayout` shell. Tables `sales_leads` (unique `phone_e164`, India `received_on`, `assigned_to` null = not allotted) and `sales_lead_events` (append-only); six browser-callable functions and two internal ones; the `create`, `edit` and `assign` actions on `sales` (`assign` is protected, per person, never by a level). The number rule (`normalize_lead_phone`, `phone.ts`) and the "sales candidate" rule (`is_eligible_order_assignee`) are reused, not copied.
- **Changed.** Sales no longer opens Coming soon (`comingSoonModules.test.tsx` expects the Lead Master for Sales); the launcher card description; `MODULE_WORDS`, `enforcement.ts` and the module registration for `sales`; the Sales row in `accessCatalog.test.ts`; both migration ledgers in `participantAndOrderTotalSecurity.test.ts`.
- **Decisions to confirm.** Only an Admin or a holder of `assign` may allot or change a name (the earlier default "whoever adds picks the person" went with the allocation). A repeat enquiry from a known number is blocked until Lead Management exists. The 2024–2026 sheet history is **not** imported by this change.
- **Proof.** An in-memory Postgres run of the real migration over stand-ins for the platform pieces, using the real phone normaliser and the real sales-candidate rule: 46 checks (two real bugs found and fixed by it: an array append that failed, and an append-only history that would have blocked deleting a person). The new unit and render tests, the whole suite, `tsc` and lint. The mock-up was rendered and read in headless Edge at 1536, 1280 and 390 px. **Not seen signed in with the built screens by the builder.**
- **After an independent review of the unseen UI code** (fixed in this change, each with a test): Escape no longer closes the lead panel together with the confirmation under it, and Tab stays inside the layer on top and gives focus back when it closes (`useDialogFocus`); a saved allotment keeps showing the new name until the fresh list is in, and one lead saving no longer clears another's question or busy state; a stray arrow key is told apart from a deliberate pick by time, not by a key-up that never arrives on a Mac; an edit started from an older version of a lead is refused instead of silently undoing someone else's edit; a refused duplicate no longer uses up a lead number; a number can be found in search however it is typed; a 0 left in front of a UAE or UK number, or an "Other" number without its +, is refused (the shared phone rule cannot see those two); a search looks through every day, not only the selected period; error messages stay up longer and are announced as alerts.
- **Migration number.** `20270523000000` is the Meta Ads weekly review (PR #592, merged), so this one is `20270524000000` and goes after it.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR; the Sales page goes back to Coming soon. The migration's header lists its rollback (drop the functions and the two tables, remove the three action rows); export the register first, because dropping the tables loses it.

## 2026-11-02 — Orders dashboard for approvers redesigned: Needs your decision, Revenue, Orders to watch (PR #585, cf79df54) · no migration

- **Changed.** For a reader who can approve PIs: **Needs your decision** is one list (PIs in review, revised PIs on confirmed Orders, Design Files / Client PO submissions) sorted by longest wait; the wait turns amber after 24 h and red after 72 h, always with an icon. Each PI has a payment chip taken from `pi_submission_payment_summary().approval_position` (one call per PI, first 12): Payment met, Exception to decide, Awaiting Finance, Payment short and so on, plus "Verified N%". It is never a percentage worked out on the page and never says "ready to approve". **Revenue** is compact; a zero month on the 2nd is a plain figure, not red; "≈ ₹22.6 L a month" is the last six completed months ÷ 6. **Orders to watch** has a pipeline bar (running · ready for production · not aligned) and five tabs (Delayed, Due in 7 days, Payments pending, Fabric / finish, Not aligned); Delayed and Due in 7 days are back.
- **Kept.** Header buttons, Factory Focus, Operations responses, every other role's dashboard. `DocumentActionQueue` gained `hideNeedsYou` and draws only "your submissions awaiting someone else" for approvers.
- **Removed.** `PiReviewSections.tsx` and the `.pi-q*` rules in globals.css. New styles are a CSS module (`adminDashboard.module.css`) that follows the container's width, so the sidebar cannot break the layout.
- **Not changed.** No SQL, no permission, no new RPC. The new reads all run under the reader's own RLS and nothing is read under View As. A failed PI read shows an error line, never "nothing waiting"; a PI whose payment summary cannot be read simply has no chip.
- **Known.** Revisions and documents are still read by two components for the rare "awaiting someone else" block. Factory Focus keeps its current card look.
- **Proof.** 34 new tests (the rules, the payment chip for all 7 positions, sorting, the pipeline bar, the three zones rendered, what the page keeps and drops); the real components rendered with the real stylesheet at 1480, 1100 and 600 wide in headless Edge and compared with the approved mockup. ## 2026-11-02 — Old orders (BDPI): a PI over 4.5 MB uploads (PR #581, 6610af35) · no migration
- **Owner report.** Uploading the PI for Order 498 (`498 chinchwad Site.xlsx`, 6.09 MiB) on Old orders (BDPI) showed "The PI could not be uploaded. Please try again." every time.
- **Cause.** The page posted the file inside the request. Vercel refuses a body over 4.5 MB with a plain-text `413 FUNCTION_PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` before the route runs, so the page had no message to show and fell back to its generic one. The workbook itself was fine: the same parser read it in 1.4 s into 9 lines and 9 pictures. Proved against production with a 3 MB dummy body (reached the route, 401) and a 5.5 MB one (413). BDPI-0001 (Order 497) had been small enough.
- **Changed.** `POST /api/orders/bdpi/import` is now two JSON calls, like `fabric-finish/refresh`: the first hands out a signed storage key to the asking person's own folder `bdpi-uploads/<user id>/` once the database's own two questions (`module_entry_open('orders')`, `actor_has_module_permission('orders','edit_payment_plan')`) say yes; the browser uploads the file to storage; the second reads it there, runs the unchanged parser and `create_bdpi` AS THE PERSON, stores the workbook and pictures under `bdpi/<id>/`, and always removes the temporary file. The page refuses a non-.xlsx, empty or over-10 MB file in words before sending anything. No limit on what a BDPI holds changed (the bucket's 10 MiB stays).
- **Behaviour note.** If the service-role key is missing the upload is refused at the first step; before, the BDPI was created without files.
- **Proof.** The real route code replayed in a scratch copy with a fake Supabase and the real parser and builder, eight scenarios: signed out 401; no permission 403 and no key handed out; step one's key under the person's own folder; another person's or a stored path refused; the 498 file end to end (BDPI created with old order 498, client Chinchor Site, 9 lines, workbook and 9 pictures stored, attach called, temporary file gone); empty and garbage files refused in words with the temporary file removed; a duplicate old order refused in words with nothing stored; a missing upload says so. `bdpiImport.test.ts` gained two tests and its ordering test now pins the gate before the service role and `create_bdpi` before any `bdpi/` write. Type-check and lint clean. Not run signed in on a preview: the browser upload to storage itself is untested here.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR; no data changes. A refused upload (unreadable file, duplicate old order, no permission) leaves nothing under `bdpi/`. A file under `bdpi-uploads/` can stay only when the tab is closed or the connection drops between the two calls; it is private, unreferenced and harmless, and can be deleted by hand.
- **Shipped.** Squash-merged on the owner's go-ahead 2026-11-02 10:36 UTC as 6610af35 (the one required check was Vercel); production deployment 7489986961 succeeded at 10:40 UTC. No backup run for this merge: the nightly one covers the day. The owner's retry of the Order 498 upload on the live page has not been seen yet.
