## 2026-11-01 — An HDFC payment is verified only against the bank; a statement creates nothing (PR #560, f8616267) · 20270516000000 → APPLIED 2026-11-01
- **Owner's process, in place of Claim, don't create.** One transfer became two payments because two sides created records: Sales, and the statement sync. Now a request is the only thing that creates a payment, and the owner verifies it against the bank credit. Design on the canvas, page Hybrid: your process.
- **The rule.** A trigger on the table, so no door or person gets round it: an HDFC PRI or SEC payment cannot turn verified unless active links in its own account cover its amount, or an admin recorded an exception. One credit cannot carry more than its amount, so a second payment for the same money can never be confirmed. Payments already verified, PNB, Paytm, Canara and test data are not touched.
- **Doors.** `verify_payment_with_bank` links and verifies in one transaction (reconcile + approve). `verify_payment_bank_check_pending` is admin only, with a reason, for money seen in the bank app before a statement is uploaded; it counts at once and stays on `finance_bank_check_pending` until credits cover it. `complete_payment_entry` leaves an unmatched HDFC entry by a self-verifier pending, where the rule would otherwise have failed the whole entry.
- **Screens.** Payment Requests: Pending has views for a reconciler (All, Match found, Waiting for bank, Bank check pending, Cash to verify), worked out over every pending payment; a PI waiting tag; the request and the credit side by side near the top of the window, with Verify on the credit. A credit that does not plainly fit never gets the red button and asks first. Bank Statements: upload and Check sync add nothing, Add to Confirmed and its window are gone, Synced reads Matched, and Record it opens the ordinary form filled in from the credit. The PI page offers Verify in Finance for an HDFC payment. Cash payments are decided as before.
- **Release.** Merged before the migration was applied, so until it was, HDFC payments could not be verified on the live app. Nothing was recorded wrongly: a refused Verify leaves the payment pending. The rehearsal passed on the real rows with nothing left behind, and the migration was the only one pending. This order is the reverse of the plan; apply first next time.
- **Not yet.** `sync_bank_credits` and `add_bank_line_to_confirmed` stay open until the owner has seen the new screens work, then a second migration closes them to browsers. UTR refusal, Verify all exact matches, one-drop upload, the cash warning, Use bank amount and the clean-up are later steps.
- **Proof.** `supabase/tests/bank_proof_before_verify_pglite.mjs`, 55 checks on the real verification, guard and link functions; 30 deliberate breaks of the migration all caught. Not yet seen in a browser or signed in.

## 2026-11-01 — Backups run once a day, not after every merge (PR #559) · no SQL
- **Owner request.** A backup after every merge wastes time and resources. Do it once in 24 hours for everything merged, and keep a record of what was merged but not yet backed up.
- **Why this is also safer.** Retention counts runs, not days. At 19 to 24 merges a day, all 14 kept database snapshots and all 30 kept bundles came from 2026-10-31 alone, so a mistake noticed the next morning had almost no history to go back to. One run a day keeps 14 days of snapshots.
- **Changed.** Sessions no longer run `backup-all.mjs` after a merge (`AGENTS.md`, Sheet §8–9, `03`, `04`, `05`, `00`). The 9:30 pm task is the backup. Hand runs remain only before a production migration, and once when the reminder says the backup is overdue.
- **The record.** A plain `backup-all.mjs` run that fully succeeds writes `status/covered.json`. New `pending-backups.mjs` compares it with GitHub and lists what is waiting, and the daily task prints that list before and after its run. A failed or partial run writes nothing, so its merges stay listed.
- **Two tools follow it.** `verify-backups` no longer fails just because main is ahead of the local mirror while the nightly backup is on time; `daily-backup-health` now reads the same record.
- **Code during the day.** The private mirror still receives every push to `main` (last 100 runs green, mirror equal to main on 2026-11-01). Only the local bundle, the database and the uploaded files wait for the night.
- **Known weakness.** Between 18 and 31 October the nightly task finished cleanly on 9 of 14 nights; post-merge runs were hiding that. A missed night is flagged after 2 days.

## 2026-10-31 — A class a screen asks for must exist: repo-wide guard, three small styling faults fixed (#553, 2f9d5795) · no SQL · deployed (deployment 7461547039), live CSS checked · backup bundle 20261031T171127Z, database 20261031T170601Z (203 tables, 100,982 rows), verified
- **Why.** #522 deleted 64 classes still in use and nothing failed, because a class name is only a string. An audit of every class and rule against all 205 commits that touched CSS found no other page hurt that way: #522 is the only accident of its kind.
- **Guard.** `src/app/stylesDefined.test.ts` reads every screen with the TypeScript parser and fails when a className names a class with no rule in `globals.css` or an inline `<style>`, or a `styles.x` its module does not define. `NO_RULE_NEEDED` lists the 25 deliberate exceptions, each with its reason, and the test fails if one goes stale. Replayed on the code before #547 it flags all 64 Founders' classes plus `is-ok` and `is-off`. About 10 seconds on the owner's PC. It cannot see names built at run time, or a rule deleted while its name survives in another rule.
- **Fixed.** `.boe-spin` never had a rule, so eight busy icons stood still (Minop, Minop Incoming, My Attendance, five Customer Reviews controls). `/attendance/requests` asked its own stylesheet for a toolbar that lives in `ui.module.css`, so the status filter and the Scheduled shift label had no row. `.od-pair` lost its main rule in #261, so the Orders dashboard's two loading placeholders stacked with no gap.
- **Removed, nothing on screen changes.** Six names with no rule left by deliberate redesigns: `order-docs-rows`, `order-sum-rows--dates`, `pi-attach-add`, `pi-order-details`, and `recent` and `board` in the Add Lead stylesheet, which wrote the word "undefined" into the class attribute.
## 2026-10-31 — Bank credit window: Move a wrongly held payment, and a doubtful match asks first (branch `fix/bank-move-payment`, not merged) · no SQL
- **Owner request.** A credit and its recorded payment were plainly the same money, and the window seemed to give no way to link them, because the payment was held by a wrong link on another credit. "Yes, build it" to the fix proposed from the diagnosis.
- **What was really wrong.** Nothing refused the fix. The right action was a quiet Unlink, then a Link in a second step, with the reason box below the next card. The only red button sat on another customer's payment 10 days away, and pressing it was accepted, read Synced, and stranded a third credit.
- **Move.** A card whose payment is held by a further credit offers Move this payment here. The reason box opens under that card, and one press sends the unlink and then the link through their own doors. A refusal at either step shows on the card, and a refused link leaves the payment free with the link offered.
- **Doubts.** `matchDoubts`: the name does not fit, the payment has no client, or the dates are more than 3 days apart. A bank reference outweighs both. The doubt is said on the card, the button is never red and reads Link anyway or Move anyway, and it asks first. Nothing else changes for a match with no doubt: one red press, as before.
- **Unchanged.** No SQL. The doors, the matches, what counts as Synced, and the verifier's bank panel, which keeps its own Link.
- **Proof.** The window driven in a DOM for every press and refusal, and ten deliberate breaks of the new code, all caught. An earlier replay of the real rows on PGlite with the merged functions proved the two doors in this order. Not seen signed in, and no live row pressed.

## 2026-10-31 — Bank credit review: Synced is checked, a person decides every possible duplicate, a wrong link is shown, sure payments link themselves (PR #544, squash-merged as 99bf7fbc, 13:03 UTC, on the owner's yes) · migration `20270514000000` → APPLIED 2026-10-31 by the owner, before the merge · Production deployment 7458973523 success 13:05 UTC · backup bundle 20261031T131734Z, database 20261031T131544Z (203 tables, 100,935 rows, restricted tables included), verified
- **Owner request.** A credit stuck as "may already be recorded" should show what it matches; link it if it is the same money, add it if not, so every HDFC PRI and SEC credit ends in sync with Confirmed Payments, with nothing duplicated or missed.
- **What two audits proved first.** Synced meant only that a link existed, so rejected, deleted, unverified, other-account, edited and part-linked payments all showed green. A text guess finished a credit with nobody deciding. The app sent people to a Link button that does not exist for a verified payment, and no Unlink button existed. A credit could be refused by both Add and Link. The Add override skipped payments nobody saw and left no record.
- **Synced.** `bank_lines_sync_state` is the one definition: verified, undeleted, same-account payments cover every rupee. Read live. Waiting for Finance is its own amber status and counts as not yet synced. Only a person's mark gives Not needed.
- **Popup.** The recorded payments the credit could be, from `bank_lines_recorded_matches` (the rule that refuses a second payment), side by side, with Same payment, link them and Different payment, add it. A reference on a payment with room left can only be linked. A reference on a fully linked payment is never added on its own and never stuck. Unlink needs a reason.
- **Add.** `add_bank_line_to_confirmed` is the one browser door: the ids compared and a reason, refused if any other payment matches by then, and the reason is written on the new payment. `create_payment_from_bank_line` is no longer callable from a browser.
- **Both directions.** A sync check across both accounts and every month, with dates no statement covers (`bank_sync_overview`). Payments not in the bank flags a look-alike and its rows open; the verifier's bank panel warns "May be a duplicate". When both counts are zero on both accounts the records equal the bank, apart from cash-mode money and credits a person marks Old order.
- **Live rows read 2026-10-31.** Reading the app signed in, at the owner's request and changing nothing, showed eight stuck HDFC PRI credits that already have verified payments, and one payment linked to the wrong credit, which left another customer's money recorded nowhere yet drawn green.
- **Wrong links.** New match kind `linked_elsewhere`: same amount, on a payment linked in full to a credit further from its date. Add and sync hold the credit, the card names the credit holding the payment, and a person unlinks it there with a reason, then links, or says it is a different payment.
- **Sync links first.** `sync_bank_credits` links before it adds when `bank_line_sure_payment` is sure: same account, same amount, within 3 days, one payment for the credit and one credit for the payment, no reference and no wrong link pointing elsewhere. The two-way rule replaces the owner-approved one-way rule of 2026-10-29: on the real rows the one-way rule linked a payment to the wrong customer. Account mismatches stay with a person, which narrows "either HDFC".
- **Renumbered.** 20270512000000 became 20270514000000, because 20270513000000 was applied first.
- **Rollout.** Rehearsal, backup, apply by the owner from the worktree (`migration list --linked` shows it on remote, nothing local-only or remote-only, dry run up to date), then the merge about ten minutes later. Add to Confirmed could not work between apply and deploy, because the old button called a door this migration closed. The first post-merge database export stopped on `notifications: terminated`; a re-run came back clean.
- **After release.** The sync check now reads 133 credits not synced and 10 payments not in the bank on HDFC PRI, and 12 credits on HDFC SEC, across every month. Nothing got worse: old credits were never synced, and guesses and unverified links no longer count. Open for the owner: move the one wrong link, link the clear credits, and decide the rest as added or Old order. Check sync adds every credit with no match as a new Suspense payment, so credits of orders not in the app need marking Old order first.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR, then run the rollback in the migration's header.
- **Proof.** `supabase/tests/bank_line_review_pglite.mjs` 37 checks on the real functions (payment door stubbed), including one statement shaped like the real rows, where six credits link and two wait; ten deliberate breaks of the new rules were each caught; the 9 older sync checks pass on the new functions; `bankReview.test.ts`, `bankSurfaces.test.ts`, and `bankLineModal.dom.test.tsx`, which drives the real window. The real components were rendered with sample data and the real stylesheet: wording, buttons and desktop and phone widths checked. After the deploy the live page was read signed in, pressing nothing: the held credit names the credit holding its payment and offers the Unlink. No Unlink or Link has been pressed on a live row yet. The owner ran the real-door rehearsal on the linked database on 2026-10-31: REHEARSAL_OK, rolled back; the live pages and the remote migration list were unchanged afterwards. Its read-only preview listed three sure links and four credits held by one wrongly linked payment, as predicted; the other three of the six clear credits carry the bank line as their reference, so sync links them by reference. The rehearsal used up four Payment IDs, because a sequence never rolls back, so the next payment skips four numbers.

With Docker started the re-run passed with all three tables dumped. Docker has to be running for them. The second copy was not made because D: was not connected.

## 2026-10-30 — Attendance machine: Today tab, and an alert when punches stop (PR #528, squash-merged as f5eb5d3b, 15:42 UTC, on the owner's yes after their preview check) · migration 20270511000000 → APPLIED 2026-10-30 (by the owner from the worktree: backup bundle 20261030T144936Z, database 20261030T145001Z verified first, dry run listed exactly this file, migration list shows it remote) · Production deployment 7442774825 success 15:57 UTC, through 52998fff (#529): Vercel made no deployment for f5eb5d3b itself, cause unknown · backup bundle 20261030T154307Z, database 20261030T154335Z (201 tables, 100,530 rows), verified
- **Owner request.** A daily screen for the machine and a warning when it stops. Mockup approved first.
- **What.** New first tab `/attendance/today` under Attendance, where the sidebar now lands: each listed person's In, Out, Hours and status for today or any day back to 2026-11-01, the machine's state on top, punches on IDs that give nobody attendance underneath. Read-only. The Overview page gains a shortcut and no longer says attendance is an Excel import.
- **Alert.** On a working day from 2026-11-01, past 11:00 IST, with no real punch reaching BOE since midnight, the machine has stopped. Today and a For you card turn red as soon as they are opened; `/api/attendance/machine-alert` (Vercel Cron, 11:05 IST, can run up to about an hour late) writes one `attendance_machine_stopped` notification per active admin, once a day. Every screen clears by itself on the next punch; the bell row stays as a record. While the machine has stopped, or on a past working day nobody punched, no-punch rows read "Can't tell yet", never absent.
- **Apply order.** 20270511000000 went first, before the preview was tested: the Attendance & Payroll feed filters on this enum value, an unknown value fails the whole feed, and previews read the production database.
- **Not changed.** Attendance writing, payroll, corrections, and the month views.
- **Phone alerts** are not on (0 Vault secrets, 0 registered devices on 2026-10-30), so nothing reaches a phone until that setup is finished.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR. The enum value cannot be dropped and does no harm.
- **Machine IDs.** Two of the IDs saved on 2026-10-30 were the wrong way round. The owner corrected them the same day, before any machine attendance existed (0 rows), as a swap by user id in one transaction. That also settled the punches on the ID that had looked like a leaver's.
- **Proof.** `machineHealth.test.ts` and `machineDayView.test.ts`, including the real reads on `MemorySupabase`.

## 2026-10-30 — Attendance machine: punches become attendance from 1 November (PR #526, squash-merged as 00e79b56, 10:27 UTC, on the owner's yes after their preview check) · no migration · Production deployment 7436109229 success 10:31 UTC · backup bundle 20261030T102810Z, database 20261030T102828Z (201 tables, 100,496 rows), verified
- **Owner decision.** The new machine is the only attendance source from 1 November 2026, with no parallel period.
- **What.** `machineDay.ts` turns a person's punches into a day by the Excel import's rules, and two taps under 5 minutes apart count as one. `machineAttendance.ts` writes `attendance_records` with `source = 'minop'` for linked, active people, never before the start date or in a locked month, and only when a row would change. The webhook does this as messages arrive; `/api/attendance/minop-sync` repairs it nightly; saving a Machine ID fills that person's days.
- **Screens.** A machine month stops at yesterday, so today never reads as absent or missing a punch; My Attendance shows today as a "so far" line. Excel import and preview refuse months from November 2026.
- **Removed.** The old processor and `MINOP_COLLECTION_ONLY`: it read a message shape the machine never sends.
- **Rollback.** Revert the PR, then delete rows with `source = 'minop'` if any were written. The payroll engine and corrections are unchanged.
- **Proof.** `machineDay.test.ts` and `machineAttendance.test.ts`, including the real reads and writes on `MemorySupabase`: first write, a re-run writing nothing, a late punch updating the same row, a locked month, a leaver's ID, a punch recovered from the raw messages.

## 2026-10-29 — Rail sidebar: no vertical movement anywhere, fluid open/close, a clean closed rail, Vendors joins the rail (PR #485, merge commit 64ee38e0) · no migration · backup 20261028T195558Z verified
- **Owner request.** The badge numbers "danced" while the menu opened, the Control Center search box was taller in the rail than open so every icon below it jumped, and the menu had to feel like an Apple interface on every module.
- **Why it moved.** In the narrow rail the hidden labels still took part in layout and wrapped (the "Find anything… Ctrl K" box became three lines tall); the notification card was `display: none` closed and inserted when open; badges changed font size, padding and position between states and animated `top`/`right`, so their digits re-rendered every frame.
- **Fix (CSS, "HOVER RAIL SIDEBAR" in `globals.css`).** The whole rail is `white-space: nowrap`; rows keep one fixed height; captions (`boe-rail-ghost`) keep their height and fade, leaving plain spacing. Only the panel width animates, on the iOS sheet curve `cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.72, 0, 1)` (open 0.44 s after a 0.1 s intent delay, close 0.32 s after 0.06 s); labels fade and drift 4 px in once the panel is a third open. Badges keep one size (18 px, 11 px digits, tabular numbers), sit on their own compositor layer, ride the row's growing edge and settle from the icon's corner (86 %, ring) to the row's centre line by transform only. A wide count grows outward into the rail instead of covering its icon. Labels never shrink, so no "…" changes mid-animation (the account label has its fixed open width). The rail no longer sticks open when a disclosure such as Product Master is expanded (only pop-up menus keep it open).
- **Clean closed rail** (owner: "collapsed and looking really dirty"). No scrollbar on the unpinned rail (it took 15 px and pushed badges onto the icons). An open sub-list (`boe-rail-tree`: Task Management's My Tasks / Assigned By Me children, Showroom's Product Master categories) is no longer a blank gap: its rule sits on the 32 px icon line and the active child shows as a terracotta tick; rows are a compact 30 px. Showroom's product search (`boe-rail-search`) keeps its box and icon. The large notification card (`boe-rail-hide`) is shown only when the menu is pinned; the bell's badge carries the count in the rail.
- **Control Center.** The search box has a fixed 36 px height and its icon on the 32 px line of the other icons (`controlCenter.module.css`, icon 14 px).
- **Vendors.** `VendorsLayout` opts in (`railShellClass`, pin button, rail labels); it was built after the rail and still had the fixed 260 px sidebar.
- **Checked.** Every sidebar element measured closed vs open on real pages of all 16 module shells (plus Task Management and Showroom with a sub-list open) (Task Management, Orders, Finance, Control Center, Modules, Attendance & Payroll, Assets, Meetings, Samples, Performance, Inventory, Image Editor, Review Workflow, Showroom admin, Exhibition Leads, Vendors): 0 move in position or height; every icon centre at 32 px. Mid-animation frames sampled on Task Management: icons fixed on every frame, badge travels monotonically.

## 2026-10-28 — Rail sidebar: badges no longer clipped, one smooth spring motion (PR #480, merge commit ee033fb4) · no migration
- **Owner request.** Count badges on the collapsed icons were still cut off, and the hover opening was not smooth; wanted an Apple-like feel on every page.
- **Cause of the clipping.** Rows had `overflow: hidden` (to trim labels) and the collapsed badge was placed at `left: 30px` of a 44 px row, so its right half was cut. Fix: rows no longer clip (the sidebar clips labels), and the badge sits at `top: 3px; right: 3px` inside the row with a surface-coloured ring.

The badge is the SAME element in both states and glides from the icon corner to the row end (it used to jump between `absolute` and `static`). Sub-lists (`boe-rail-ghost`) and the notification card (`boe-rail-hide`) fade instead of snapping. A row with a count reserves 42 px when open and trims a long label with an ellipsis. Reduced-motion switches all of it off.
- **Files.** CSS only: "HOVER RAIL SIDEBAR" in `globals.css`. Checked in a real browser with the compiled CSS on a mock sidebar (badges 4, 18, 123; with and without a count); not yet seen on the live pages.

## 2026-10-28 — Backups: pg_dump step for the tables the service role cannot read (PR pending) · no migration
- **Owner request.** Close the gap: five relations (three tables, `order_advance_exceptions`, `order_advance_exception_voids` and `order_advance_holds`, and two views, `finance_payment_destinations` and `finance_received_payments`) were never in the export because the service role is denied them. They were covered only by Supabase's own daily backups.
- **What.** `backup-database.mjs` step 2b: when `BACKUP_DATABASE_URL` (the Supabase session-pooler URI, with the database password) is in `backups\.env.backup` and Docker is running, the three real tables are dumped with `pg_dump` inside a container into `pg-dump-restricted-tables.sql.gz.enc` (same encryption as the rest). The two views are rebuilt from exported tables, not dumped (a first live run on 2026-10-28 aborted because one of them was treated as a table; fixed). `BACKUP_PG_IMAGE` (optional) picks the image; its `pg_dump` must be at least the server's major version (production is Postgres 17.6; the image `public.ecr.aws/supabase/postgres:17.6.1.171` is already on the owner's machine, so nothing needs downloading). `verify-backups.mjs` now shows `pg_dump: …` for the covered tables.
- **Safe by design.** Any pg_dump problem (no URL, Docker off, wrong password, a table missing from the dump) is a warning only: the tables stay listed as NOT COVERED and the daily backup still succeeds. The password is redacted from log lines.
- **Found and fixed on the first live runs (2026-10-28).** (1) The database password contains an `@`, which must be written `%40` in the connection address. (2) `finance_payment_destinations` is a view, not a table, so dumping it made the whole step look failed: both views are now listed as "rebuilt from exported tables" instead of "NOT COVERED" (their sources are in the export). (3) The row counter used a pattern that, for an EMPTY table followed by other data, ran into the next table's block and recorded 9 rows for `order_advance_exception_voids` (really 0); it is now a line-based `countCopyRows` in `lib.mjs` with a test (`scripts/backup/lib.test.mjs`) that covers exactly that case.
- **Proof.** Live run: `pg_dump OK` for the three tables; the dump was decrypted and read back (every table has its CREATE and COPY block; the row counts match the manifest); verify-backups passes. Earlier: syntax checked; the container call proven with a fake host (clean failure, nothing printed but the host error); the row-count parsing run on a sample dump (2 rows, an empty table as 0, a missing table detected). Ran against the live database with the owner's `BACKUP_DATABASE_URL` (see above).

## 2026-10-28 — Every module: sidebar rests as an icon rail, opens over the page on hover (PR #475, merge commit 241398cb) · no migration
- **Owner request.** Give page content more room: the left menu is minimised and opens to the full menu on hover. Chosen: the menu OVERLAYS the page (content never moves, so a table never reflows when the pointer brushes the edge); "push content" was rejected.
- **Mechanism.** A shell opts in with `railShellClass(pinned)` (`boe-app-shell boe-rail`) from `src/components/layout/RailControls.tsx`. Only on >= 768 px with `hover: hover` and `pointer: fine` does the sidebar rest at 64 px and open to 260 px after a 150 ms hover delay, on keyboard focus inside it, while one of its own menus is open (account menu), or when pinned. Touch screens, tablets and the phone drawer (<= 767 px) are unchanged. CSS: "HOVER RAIL SIDEBAR" at the end of `globals.css`.
- **Pin.** A "Pin menu open" row at the foot keeps the full menu open and pushes the content as before; stored per browser in `localStorage['boe.sidebar.pinned']`.
- **Classes.** `boe-rail-label` (fades while collapsed), `boe-rail-hide` (removed while collapsed: the notification card), `boe-rail-ghost` (invisible but keeps its height: captions, expanded sub-lists, product search, so nothing moves when the menu opens), `boe-nav-count` (count badge moves onto the icon). Shared pieces converted once: `ShellNavLink`, `ShellHomeLink`, `NotificationsNavItem`, `UserMenu`, `PiFormatNavLink`, `QuickActions`, `ProductMasterNav`; every module layout converted (Orders, Finance, Task Management, Modules/Home, Attendance & Payroll, Control Center, Assets, Meetings, Samples, Performance, Inventory, Image Editor, Exhibition Leads, Review Workflow, Showroom).
- **Traps.** Icons sit at 32 px in both states (10 px section padding + 13 px item padding + half the 18 px icon): a new nav item needs `padding-left: 13px` under `.boe-rail`. Modals stay above the sidebar (z-index 100). A new layout must opt in or it keeps the full 260 px sidebar. In a worktree whose `node_modules` is a junction, Turbopack refuses to run (use `next dev --webpack`; a build needs a temporary wider `turbopack.root`); `next build --webpack` fails on an old `src/app/orders/layout.tsx` export that Turbopack accepts.
- **Proof.** tsc, eslint, full `npm test` (18217 pass, 0 fail), `next build`. Orders and Task Management seen on screen by the owner (icon alignment and vertical shifting fixed after review); the other modules not individually seen.
- **Released.** Merged by the owner 2026-10-28 11:26 UTC; Production deployment `success` 11:28 UTC. Backup run and verified straight after (database export 99853 rows; the external-drive copy is still not configured). Rollback: revert PR #475.

## 2026-10-27 — Orders: Documents card trimmed, respond-and-resubmit with remarks, an approver's own PI revision approves itself, Edit PI shows the order's own product code (PR pending) · migration 20270422000000 (NOT APPLIED)
- **Documents card.** The header keeps only its title: the Document history link and the Update documents menu are gone from the card. The "Clarification needed" badge and the "cannot accept … Reason: …" line are gone from the Main PI row; the same sentence now fills the detail of the top notification ("PI V2 flagged by operations", `operationsReview.reasonLine`). **Consequence:** the page no longer offers a door to upload Design Files / Client PO or a new PI workbook from the Documents card (Edit PI still proposes an in-app revision).
- **An approver's own revision.** When the person proposing a revision holds `orders.approve_order`, the revision is approved by them in the same step: `/api/orders/pi-edits` calls `approve_order_pi_revision` right after `propose_order_pi_edit_revision` (service role, actor = the proposer), and the workbook-upload door on the Order page calls `/api/orders/pi-revisions/approve` after `propose_order_pi_revision`. The version is in force at once and Operations gets its handoff. Anything that refuses leaves the version pending, as before. The Edit PI page returns `?edit_pi=in_force`. No SQL changed; the database already let an approver approve what they proposed.
- **Edit PI Code field.** On a Confirmed Order the Code field shows the Order's own code (`537-BE0001`), read-only, instead of the uploaded workbook's cell (`538-B001`, copied from a sheet whose order number was one ahead). The stored `source_product_code` is untouched. Drafts keep the editable field.
- **Dates.** The first-PI dates fix below ships in the same PR.
- **Respond and resubmit, remarks only.** New `respond_order_handoff_clarification(handoff, remark)` (migration `20270422000000_order_handoff_clarification_response.sql`; three nullable columns on `order_operations_handoffs`, one CHECK, one function): the PI owner or an active admin answers Operations' objection; the same version goes back to `awaiting`, the objection is kept, the reviewer is notified, the Order history records both. UI: **Respond and resubmit** on the top notification. The answer is shown on the page from the Order's history (`latestHandoffAnswer`), not from the new columns, so the page works whether or not the migration is applied; only the **Respond and resubmit** button needs it (without it the button's call fails with a plain message). Apply the migration before announcing the feature. Rollback is in the migration header. Rehearsed 2026-10-27 in a rolled-back transaction on the live database (six cases, zero residue); not applied.
- **Proof.** tsc clean; the changed suites and the new `operationsHandoffResponse.test.ts` pass. Not seen on screen; the self-approval and the new function have not been exercised against a live database.

## 2026-10-27 — Orders: dates timeline no longer shows approval before upload (PR pending) · no migration
- **The defect.** On an Order revised after its first approval, the timeline took the PI upload from the version now in force (V2, uploaded 25 Oct) and the approval from the first "PI approved" event (V1, 20 Oct), so "Approved" appeared before "PI uploaded". Same cause for the confirm-to-upload gap banner and the "Uploaded N days ago" line.
- **The fix.** Both dates come from the FIRST PI version (`firstPiMilestones`, `lib/orders/orderPiMilestones.ts`): V1's upload, V1's decision (the first "PI approved" event only if V1 carries none). Later revisions keep their own history in PI history and Activity.
- **Proof.** `orderPiMilestones.test.ts`; the page is pinned to use it. Not seen on the live Order.

Tabs All / PI edits / Payments / Approvals (`orderActivityCategory.ts`); a chosen tab shows every event of its kind, minor ones included.

## 2026-10-27 — Bank Statements: sync credits into Confirmed Payments (PR #465) · migration 20270423000000 APPLIED 2026-10-27
- **Auto-sync.** Uploading a statement adds each new customer credit to Confirmed Payments through `sync_bank_credits(uuid[])` (reuses `create_payment_from_bank_line`: same duplicate refusals; verified at once only for an admin / verify_own_payment holder). A credit whose bank reference is already on a same-amount unlinked payment is linked to it instead; a same-amount payment within 21 days is NOT added (shows Not Synced with the reason). One bad line never stops the others. Failures are kept on the line (`sync_error`, `sync_attempted_at`).
- **Page.** Synced / red Not Synced / Not needed column; cards All, Not synced, Synced, Old orders, Not customer money; **Check sync** button for the month with a list of what is still Not Synced (each with Add to Confirmed). Linking and unlinking a payment, suggested payments and payment search are removed from this page; credits marked or looking like non-customer money are never auto-added. Only the credits of the upload are synced on upload; older credits sync when Check sync is pressed.
- Proof: PGlite suite 9/9, then rehearsed 2026-10-27 on the live database inside a rolled-back transaction through the REAL payment door as an admin (two credits created and linked, a possible duplicate refused with its reason, a second run reported already synced, zero residue). Applied alone 2026-10-27 (20270422000000, another PR's migration, is still unapplied). Not yet seen on screen.

## 2026-10-25 — Minop: answer Transaction Data with a per-transaction acknowledgement (PR pending) · no migration
- **Why.** Minop's Developer Dashboard documents the Transaction Data reply as `{"transStatus":[{"txnId":21,"status":1}]}`. The path-token receiver answered every message with `{"status":1}`, which names no transaction. Production showed the symptom: 196 stored copies of 14 distinct punches (each re-sent ~14 times), every Minop transaction `IsSync = False`, nothing new delivered since 13 Oct.
- **What.** `minopSuccessReply()` in `src/lib/minop/pathTokenWebhook.ts`: a `trans[]` body gets a `transStatus` entry per transaction (txnId echoed in the type sent; entries without a txnId are skipped); any other body (Say Hello) still gets `{"status":1}`. Failures are unchanged (500 when not stored, 400 invalid JSON, empty 404 for a wrong token).
- **Not changed.** Storage, auth, collection-only mode, the header-authenticated route.
- **Proof.** `src/lib/minop/*.test.ts` 114 pass; tsc and lint clean. NOT verified: that Minop then marks transactions synced and resumes pushing; that needs a deploy and a live punch.

## 2026-10-24 — Finance: "Not received" is final; handovers carry whole payments only (PR #444 merged, 1e5e1e52) · migration 20270418000000 (APPLIED 2026-10-24, before merge) · backup 20261024T070013Z
- **Owner request.** Phase 2 of the cash handover simplification: accept the whole handover or refuse it with a reason; no partial receipt; a new handover is possible after a refusal.
- **What.** Marking a handover "Not received" (stored status `disputed`) now ends it: it can no longer be accepted, cancelled, reminded or answered again, and nobody has to cancel it. Custody never moved, so the payment is back with the giver, who can hand it over again at once; the refusal and its reason stay on record next to the later handover. Everywhere "open" now means pending only (banner, lists, the Phase 1 lock, reservations, reminders, direct receipt). `create_cash_handover` refuses a typed cash amount; a handover carries whole payments only. Acceptance still never approves a payment (20270417000000 body kept).
- **Screens.** "Dispute" became "Not received" (menu, dialog, notification label, journey wording); history has a Not received tab and counts it as closed.
- **Not changed.** Table, statuses, events, notification types, RLS, grants, custody history, cash-balance tables, Finance verification, order approval.
- **Existing records.** None affected: production has 0 disputed, 0 pending, 0 typed-cash handovers and 0 cash-balance rows (census 2026-10-24).
- **Proof.** PGlite `supabase/tests/handover_not_received_final_pglite.mjs`: real migrations 20270410 → 20270418, 14 checks pass (refusal needs the recipient and a reason; final; retry with full history; duplicate acceptance refused; whole amounts and one-argument accept; amount frozen while open; typed cash refused; lock follows; reminders; direct receipt; order-approval check; privileges); the negative control without the migration fails. Each restated body diffed against its source: only the intended lines differ.
- **Owner steps.** Done: 20270418000000 APPLIED to production 2026-10-24 by `supabase db push` (owner approved; backup 20261024T064358Z first; dry run listed only this file; main unchanged; no other PR used the version). Checked after, read-only: in history; all 14 bodies present and none treats disputed as open; guard closes disputed; typed cash refused; plain-words message live; acceptance still never approves (0 auto-confirmed payments); grants unchanged (anon none, signed-in may answer, lock helper internal); one create_cash_handover signature; payment lock trigger bound; 0 pending/disputed handovers; P-AA-0044 still locked and Pending. Merged 2026-10-24 (rebase, 1e5e1e52) after CI green; Production deploy `success`; CI and "Backup on merge" green on the merge commit; post-merge backup 20261024T070013Z, verify-backups "All required layers are fresh". On screen (production, as Nishant): the Handover history "Not received" tab is live; the Not received action and dialog were not exercised (needs a handover addressed to the viewer).

## 2026-10-24 — Finance: accepting a cash handover confirms its payments (PR open) · migration 20270416000000 (APPLIED 2026-10-24, before merge; checked: in history, function confirms, anon closed, dry run up to date)
- **Owner request.** After a recipient accepted a handover, the payment page still asked for the payment to be verified again. Receiving the cash is the verification.
- **What.** `accept_cash_handover` also confirms each Pending payment in the handover through `approve_finance_payment_request` (same door, same checks and allocation intents), in the same transaction, only when the person accepting holds Finance approve. ## 2026-10-23 — List pagination: shared pager + Sample Tracking paged (branch feat/list-pagination, PR open) · no migration
- **Why.** Lists that download a whole table and filter in the browser are silently clipped at PostgREST's 1000-row cap and slow down with every row added. Audit found ~30 such screens.
- **Shared pieces.** `src/lib/listPaging.ts` (30-row pages, ranges, page strip), `usePagedQuery` (keeps the previous page on screen within the same scope, reads the next page early), `components/ui/Pagination.tsx` (renders nothing at 30 rows or fewer; prefetches on hover/focus).
- **Sample Tracking.** The page no longer reads the whole table. Each tab is a status filter on one 30-row page (`src/lib/samples/listQuery.ts`, `useSampleRequests.ts`); tab counts are database head counts; an action invalidates cached pages instead of re-downloading everything. Dispatched tab now orders by earliest expected return (overdue first). Visibility rule unchanged (admin or any sample permission sees all, else own requests).
- **Proof.** tsc, eslint, listPaging + samples listQuery unit tests, existing Samples permission suites. NOT seen on screen.
- **Next.** Orders (All) needs a view/RPC for value sort and name search, then My Tasks, Customer Reviews test cards, Inventory, the rest of the audit list.

## 2026-10-23 — Load time: the Order header draws without waiting for the combined read

- **What changed.** `loadOrder` on the Order page now starts the Order's own row read at the same moment as the combined `order_page_bundle` read, and draws the header from whichever lands first. The combined read then replaces the same values and brings the rest of the page. A flag (`bundleApplied`) stops a row that lands late from overwriting what the combined read already put on screen; if the combined read is unavailable, the fallback path reuses the early row instead of reading it again. No migration, no permission change: the row is read under the caller's own row-level security exactly as before.
- **Cost.** Two more database requests per open (the row and its total are read twice when the combined read also answers): about 17 instead of 15.
- **Measured on the live page** (same click-through test as #413, warm runs, signed in): see the PR description for before and after.

## 2026-10-23 — Admin "Login as member": floating icon, real session swap (PR #421, merge f154f397) · migration 20270409000000 (APPLIED 2026-10-23)
- **Owner request.** An admin must be able to open any member's app exactly as they see it and act for them, to test new features; members change their passwords, so the admin cannot log in normally. No filters or limits.
- **What.** A floating icon (`LoginAsMemberWidget`, mounted once from `Providers`, admin-only, hidden on login pages) lists EVERY non-deleted member (inactive flagged). Choosing one calls `POST /api/impersonation/start` (active admin only; audits first; mints a one-time magic-link token, no email sent), the browser stashes the admin session in localStorage (`boeAdminImpersonation`) and exchanges the token with `verifyOtp`. From then on RLS, permissions, notifications, writes behave as that member. An amber "Acting as X" pill and a Return button stay on screen; switching member returns to admin first. `POST /api/impersonation/end` closes the audit row (caller must be the target). `GET /api/impersonation/members` is the admin-gated list.
- **Replaces.** The per-layout "Switch User" read-only dropdown in `ViewModeSidebarSection` is removed. The read-only View As preview (`viewAs.ts`, Team Performance "full report") is untouched.
- **Audit.** `admin_impersonation_sessions` (admin, target, started/ended, ip, user agent); RLS on, no policies, no grants to browser roles. Actions taken as a member are real and attributed to the member; they are NOT tagged.
- **Traps.** Never call `signOut()` during a swap (revokes the stashed admin refresh token). The stash is written BEFORE `verifyOtp`. Admin refresh tokens are single-use: `freshAdminToken` refreshes through an isolated client and writes the rotated tokens back. Emails/WhatsApp triggered while acting as a member go to real recipients.
- **Proof.** tsc, eslint, `impersonation.test.ts` + related View As suites. NOT seen on screen; the swap itself needs the migration applied and a real login.
- **Owner steps.** Apply migration 20270409000000, then test on a real member.
- **Rollback.** Drop the table; revert the PR.

## 2026-10-22 — Allocate a payment to an OLD order that is not in the system (PR open) · migration 20270407000000 (NOT yet applied)
- **Owner request.** Payments often arrive for old orders that were never entered here, so Allocate Funds needs a custom option: type the old order number and the client name.
- **What.** A third kind of allocation target: a **legacy order**, stored on `finance_payment_allocations` as `legacy_order_no` + `legacy_client_name` with neither `order_id` nor `order_submission_id`. It is a real allocation (the payment's allocated total, balance and Zero / Partially / Fully Allocated count it) but no Order or PI figure ever reads it, so it cannot reach the 40% gate. One active allocation per payment per old order number (number compared trimmed, spaces collapsed, upper case); reversal keeps the row as before. Allocate Funds and Record Payment (under the Confirmed Order destination only) get an "Old order (not in system)" button per row beside "Choose an Order or PI Draft"; Allocated Against, the allocation popup, the detail panel, Correct Allocation, the payment history and Delete Payment name it ("Old order AB-12 · Client"), with no link. The mixed-customer warning counts the typed client.
- **Database.** Columns + constraints (`one_target` now counts the third kind, `legacy_shape`, origin `legacy_order`), unique index, `normalize_legacy_order_no()`, `allocate_payment_to_legacy_order_internal()` (closed to clients). Re-emitted bodies, each with only the named additions: `finance_payment_allocations_guard_transition` (legacy columns immutable), `log_finance_payment_allocation_activity` (payload names the old order), `allocate_payment_to_targets` (accepts `{legacy_order_no, legacy_client_name, allocated_amount}`), `record_payment_with_allocations_core` (WRAPPED, not rewritten: the deployed core is renamed `..._base` and a thin core of the same signature takes `{kind:'legacy', order_no, client_name, amount}` rows out of the list, lets the base record the payment and every Order/PI row, then allocates the old-order rows in the same transaction and re-derives the display customer over all targets; the idempotency wrapper above is untouched), `received_payment_allocation_targets` (return type gains `target_client_name`; dropped and re-created with the same grants), `payment_allocation_ledger_for_correction` (names legacy rows). **Not touched:** `finance_received_payments` (it already totals every active allocation), `finance_payment_destinations`, RLS policies, every Order/PI total. Permission unchanged: `finance.allocate` to create, `finance.allocate_correct` to reverse.
- **Proof.** `supabase/tests/run_allocate_old_order_suite.sh` on a disposable Postgres 16 (docker): the shaped schema with the real 20260918 table constraints and the deployed capacity / reversal / transition / activity bodies, then the migration, then assertions (normalised write, audit payload, duplicate / over-capacity / blank client / amount refusals, atomic rollback of a mixed call, constraints and immutability for direct writers, reversal then corrected re-allocation, totals count the legacy row and an Order's does not, both readers). `legacyOrderAllocation.test.ts` (31 tests), tsc, eslint, full suite. **Not run against the real schema or live data, and not seen on screen.**
- **Known limits.** A payment whose only destination is an old order still reads as having no PI/Order destination in `finance_payment_destinations` (Payment Requests "Payment Against"). `edit_payment_request` cannot change the amount of a pending payment whose single allocation is an old order (reverse it in Correct Allocation first). Bank Statements' own "old order" mark is a separate thing and is unchanged.
- **Owner steps.** Back up, then apply 20270407000000 with `db push --linked` BEFORE merging (Allocate Funds and Record Payment send the new target shape and the readers name the new column). Merged first, an old-order allocation would be refused by the old function and the list would still work.
- **Rollback.** Reverse any old-order allocations in Correct Allocation, then re-emit the previous bodies of the five functions; the columns can stay.

## 2026-10-22 — Load time: the Order page reads once, not four times in a row (PR #413, merged 681d376d) · migration 20270406000000 (APPLIED 2026-10-22)
- **Measured (live, admin).** Opening an Order: header about 725 ms, whole page about 1.6 s. The page read the order, then fourteen things about its PI, payments and history, then the people and the photo links, then the whole-payment totals: four rounds in a row, about a quarter of a second each, and requests sent together finish in clusters of about six, so the fourteen took two clusters.
- **What.** `public.order_page_bundle(order_id)`, one read-only function returning everything the page reads once it knows the Order's id as one jsonb answer (order row with its people, advance readiness; the source PI: submission, items, images, codes, versions, trail, handoffs, commission and whether it may be read; payments, allocations, active totals; activity, approvals, change requests; the names all of those mention). **SECURITY INVOKER**: every table's own row-level security decides, exactly as for the separate reads. Each part is `{ok, data}` or `{ok:false, code}` so one refused part cannot take the others down; an Order the reader cannot see answers `{found:false}`. The page keeps all its separate reads as the fallback (function absent, refused, or an unexpected answer), remembered per tab so a database without the function is asked once. After the bundle only the signed photo links remain (storage cannot be called from the database).
- **Proof.** The function and the actual migration file were executed inside BEGIN..ROLLBACK against the live database for all eight Orders as an administrator (225 checks), an operations user (224) and a salesperson who sees one of the eight (35): 0 failed (row counts against each reader's own reads, column sets against the page's own constants, visibility, every part working); about 100 ms inside the database, about 24 KB an Order; nothing left behind. tsc, eslint, a real `next build`, full suite, new tests (`orderPageBundle.test.ts`, `orderPageBundleSchema.test.ts`).
- **A defect found on the way (fixed here).** The page issued its five reads as payments, allocations, activity, APPROVALS, change requests but named the results aData, cData, apprData, so the approvals state was filled from the change requests and the change requests from the approvals. Invisible today (both tables are empty: 0 approval events, 0 change requests), but the first recorded approval would have vanished from the Fabric and Finish card on the next full page load and appeared in the change-request list. The names now follow the order of the reads, in both paths, and a test pins it.
- **Applied and merged 2026-10-22** on the owner's instruction: fresh backup, dry run showed only this file, single-file `db push`, history shows it in `remote`, then the PR was merged and deployed. **Measured on the live page afterwards** (same click-through test, warm runs, admin): whole page about 1,261 ms before (1,512 / 1,149 / 1,612 / 1,261 / 1,091) and about 865 ms after (877 / 2,180 / 882 / 830 / 863 / 817), database requests per open 34 before and 16 after. The header is NOT faster: about 524 ms before, about 540-800 ms after, because it now waits for the one combined read and appears together with the rest of the page. Runs vary a lot (the first run after every deploy is slow; one run took 12 s). Treat the whole-page gain as roughly 30%.
- **Release order (kept for the record).** Migration first (the owner applies), then merge. Merged first, the page would make one failed call per tab and then read the old way (slower than today, not broken).
- **Rollback.** `drop function public.order_page_bundle(uuid);` (the page falls back by itself).

## 2026-10-22 — Deleting a Confirmed Payment that was applied from an intent (PR open) · migration 20270405000000 (APPLIED 2026-10-22; backup 20261022T101107Z)
- **Report.** Deleting P-AA-0040 kept showing "Payment deletion did not finish". The production log (added by #408) named the cause: `finance_payment_allocation_intents_applied_allocation_id_fkey`.
- **Cause.** A payment entered with a destination has an intent whose `applied_allocation_id` names its allocation. The BEFORE DELETE release trigger deletes the allocations while that intent still points at one; the cascade to the intents runs only afterwards. The earlier storage theory (#408) was not the cause, though that change is harmless.
- **Database.** `finalize_finance_payment_deletion` re-emitted from 20270331000000 §4 by script plus one statement: delete the payment's intents before the payment. Signature, grants, authorization, claim, bank-link rule unchanged. No table, column, policy or permission change.
- **Proof.** Source-level test `paymentDeletionClearsIntents.test.ts`, plus a run on a disposable Postgres 16 (docker): with the shaped schema, the deployed 20261011/20261218 bodies and 20270331 applied, an admin's finalize on a payment with an applied intent FAILED with the exact production error; after applying 20270405000000 the same finalize succeeded (payment, intent gone; claim finalized; 1 allocation released). Not run against the real schema or live data.
- **Owner steps.** Done 2026-10-22: backed up, applied 20270405000000 with `db push --linked`, and deleting P-AA-0040 now works (owner confirmed on screen).
- **Rollback.** Re-emit the 20270331000000 §4 body.

## 2026-10-22 — Load time: Order and PI Draft pages are prepared once and cached (PR open) · no migration
- **Measured (live, admin).** Opening an Order took about 770 ms warm (runs 668, 924, 887, 772, 636; the header at about 0.7-1.2 s, everything at about 1.4-2.4 s on slower runs). The first data request left about 500 ms after the click, where PI Drafts leaves after about 100 ms. Cause: `/orders/[id]` and `/orders/drafts/[submissionId]` were dynamic routes (`ƒ` in the build), so every open ran a server function in Tokyo to fetch the page itself (`x-vercel-cache: MISS`, `no-store`, about 260-400 ms; the first call after idle took 10.6 s: a cold start) before the browser could ask for any data, and Next does not prefetch a dynamic page. No main-thread blocking was found (no long tasks).
- **What.** A server `layout.tsx` in each of the two record folders exports an empty `generateStaticParams` (Next: "all paths at runtime"). Each record page is prepared the first time it is asked for and then served from the cache, so the link's hover prefetch really fetches it and an open starts its data reads at once. The build now marks `/orders/[id]`, `/orders/[id]/edit-pi`, `/orders/drafts/[submissionId]` and `.../edit-pi` as `●`.
- **Why it is safe.** Both pages are client components; the server output has no order or draft data (it arrives from the database in the browser, scoped by RLS). There is no middleware. A test pins that nothing under the two routes (or the orders and root layouts) reads cookies or headers or forces dynamic rendering; if that ever changed, Next would refuse to cache and the page would just go back to dynamic.
- **Not changed.** Who sees what; any query. Other dynamic record routes (tasks, requests) are untouched.
- **Rollback.** Delete the two `layout.tsx` files.
- **Proof.** tsc, eslint, full suite, a real `next build` (routes show `●`), a new test (`recordPagesCached.test.ts`). After-numbers in the PR.

## 2026-10-22 — Payment delete: a proof file already gone from storage no longer blocks deletion (PR open) · no migration
- **Report.** Deleting Confirmed Payment P-AA-0040 showed "Payment deletion did not finish. Retry deletion." on every retry.
- **Likely cause (inferred from code, not seen in the live logs).** `/api/finance/payments/delete` counted a proof key as removed only when `storage.remove()` echoed it back. Storage returns nothing for a key that is not in the bucket, so a manifest entry with no object could never be confirmed and every retry ended in `STORAGE_INCOMPLETE`, which shares the generic message.
- **What.** When the sweep looks short, the route lists the payment's own folder and counts keys that are absent as removed; keys still listed stay outstanding. begin / remove / finalize errors are now logged server-side (`[payment-delete]`).
- **Not changed.** Who may delete, the claim protocol, the database, the messages.
- **Proof.** Finance tests pass. NOT exercised against live storage; if the retry still fails, the new server log names the real error.

## 2026-10-22 — Load time: PI Drafts in one round trip (PR open) · migration 20270404000000 (NOT APPLIED — waits for the owner)
- **Measured first (live, admin).** A database call costs about 230-270 ms although the database answers in under 1 ms: about 63 ms network, about 45 ms for the browser's cross-origin check (uncached), and a fixed cost on the Supabase side. So a screen's time is the number of sequential rounds. PI Drafts took about 600 ms in steady state (932 and 1070 ms on the first two runs): one round for rows, profile and permissions, then a second for the advance percentages of those ids.
- **What.** `public.attached_percent(order_submissions)`, a PostgREST computed column that calls the existing batch function `pi_submissions_advance_percent()` (so it is the same figure and the same visibility rule, not a third copy). The list asks for `attached_percent` in its select, so rows and percentages arrive together. A database without the migration refuses the column by name and the page reads the list the old two-step way, so deploy order cannot break the screen.
- **Not changed.** Profile and permission reads stay fresh on every load; the module guards still wait for their answer. The permission cache the owner approved was NOT built: the measurements showed those reads already run in the same round as the data, so a cache would not shorten the screen.
- **Proof.** tsc, eslint, full test suite; migration executed in BEGIN…ROLLBACK against the linked DB, including a check that the computed column equals the batch function for every row. After-numbers are recorded in the PR once the migration is applied.
- **Rollback.** `drop function public.attached_percent(public.order_submissions);` (the page falls back by itself).

## 2026-10-22 — Load time: open the database connection during page load (PR open) · no migration
- **Why.** Every screen reads from the browser, so the first query of each page load first opened a connection (DNS + TCP + TLS, about three round trips) only after the scripts had booted. Measured on the live site (admin, warm): HTML ready in about 75 ms, first query not sent until about 245-285 ms, and the first answer back at about 480-780 ms; the database round trip itself is about 63 ms and the queries run in a few ms inside the database, so the waiting is connection setup and the chain of reads, not the database.
- **What.** `src/app/layout.tsx` adds `preconnect(NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, { crossOrigin: 'anonymous' })` (anonymous because the data client's cross-origin fetches carry no cookies and use their own connection pool). A hint only: no URL, no hint.
- **Not changed (and why).** The module guards still hold back their children until the access check answers: that is a recorded security decision, not an oversight. Per-page permission/profile reads are still fresh per load (pinned by tests). Further gains need an owner decision on short-lived caching.
- **Proof.** tsc, eslint, full test suite (17,778 tests, 0 failures). Gain not separable in a warm browser (the connection is already open); it is for the first load after idle and for phones.

- **Rollout:** backed up (2026-10-21 17:29 UTC) → owner applied 20270331000000 (dry-run showed only this file; remote column confirmed) → merged as #391 (792b070b, 2026-10-21 17:21 UTC), Production deployment success, Backup on merge green, local backup 17:45 UTC. Rollback: a new migration re-emitting the 20261218 (deletable_by, guard) and 20261011 (begin, finalize) bodies.

## 2026-10-21 — Quotation Details redesigned: request on the left, the quotation you send on the right (PR open) · no migration
- **Owner request.** A quotation is not a task: sales asks, the quotation team answers with a quote. The page no longer reuses the Task Detail layout.
- **What.** For a live quotation task `/tasks/[id]` renders `QuotationDetailView`: status strip, "What sales asked for" (lead, phone, requester, requirement, sales' files), and a "Send Quotation to <sales person>" card that needs at least one quote file (optional note). Sending records an update tagged `quotation_sent` (in `task_activity_log.new_val`, no schema change) then completes the task; the sales person gets the existing completion notice only. After sending: "Quotation sent" card + "Reopen for revision". "Updates" became "Message sales"; "Quotation History" became "Timeline". Ordinary tasks and cancelled quotations are untouched.
- **Code.** `saveComment` now returns a boolean; the activity card was hoisted into one `activityCard` constant shared by both layouts. Tests: `quotationSend.test.ts`; 1,609 task/notification/meeting tests green, tsc + eslint clean. Not yet checked on screen in a signed-in session.

## 2026-10-21 — Task attachment preview: Download works again and keeps the original filename (PR open) · no migration
- **Problem:** in the Task/Quotation attachment pop-up, Download did nothing on a large workbook, and Open in Tab saved it under the generated storage key.
- **Cause:** Download fetched the file into a blob and revoked the blob URL in the same tick as the click (can cancel a big save silently, response never checked). Open in Tab used the plain signed URL, which carries no filename.
- **Fix:** `signAttachmentUrl(..., downloadAs)` asks storage for Content-Disposition with the original name. The task page signs a second URL with it; Download asks for a fresh one on click (links live 300 s) and falls back to a checked blob download that revokes after 60 s; Open in Tab uses it for non-image, non-PDF files. The preview still renders from the plain URL. Test: `attachmentDownload.test.ts`. Not yet confirmed on screen in a signed-in session.

## 2026-10-21 — Finance: Bank Statements — upload HDFC statements, link bank credits to payments (PR open) · migration 20270329000000 (NOT APPLIED)
- **Owner request.** Upload the company's bank statements (HDFC PRI main account and SEC petty-cash account) at intervals; see which credits are linked to a recorded payment and which are not; get a suggested match for a payment ("maybe the date changed, but this looks like it") without anything being approved automatically; record credits that belong to old orders never put into the app.
- **What.** New page `/finance/bank` (sidebar "Bank Statements", only for holders of the new protected `finance.reconcile`; admins automatically). Upload drop zone → server-side read (`/api/finance/bank/parse`, writes nothing) → preview with checks (every balance adds up to the paisa, summary totals match, opening balance continues the stored closing balance, N new vs N already stored) → Import. Credits listed with status cards that filter (Unlinked / Has a suggestion / Linked / Old orders / Not customer money); line dialog with ranked suggestions and reasons, search for another payment, Old order form, Not customer money form; tabs for Payments not in the bank and Money out.
- **Migration 20270329000000** adds `bank_statement_accounts/imports/lines`, `bank_line_payment_links`, six RPCs, `finance.reconcile` (deny-by-default). Tables are readable by reconcilers only and writable by RPC only. No account number is in the repository: the first import records it in the database.
- **Linking never verifies, rejects, edits or allocates a payment** (pinned by `bankSurfaces.test.ts`).
- **Proof.** tsc, eslint, 2,481 finance + permissions tests green, `next build --webpack` compiled. Migration executed in a throwaway Postgres (PGlite) with the real September + October statements for both accounts: 312 + 60 lines, credits/debits equal the statements' summaries, re-uploading October added 0, outsider refused, direct table writes refused. NOT yet seen on screen in a signed-in session; `permissions:check` not run (needs production credentials; it will report `finance.reconcile` until the migration is applied).
- **Rollout order:** back up → owner applies 20270329000000 (`db push --dry-run` first; only this file pending) → merge. Rollback: drop the four tables and six functions and delete the `reconcile` rows; nothing else references them.

## 2026-10-21 — Real Orders and Finance data re-labelled as real, not test (PR open) · migration 20270328000300 (APPLIED to production 2026-10-21)
- **Why:** Test Data Cleanup was enabled from 2026-07-21, so every Order and payment created since go-live (2026-10-18) was stamped `is_test_data = true`; the dashboard, revenue and Factory Focus hid them and the cleanup/reset tools would have treated them as deletable. The owner **permanently disabled** the phase in the Control Center on 2026-10-21 11:52 UTC (verified: `permanently_disabled = true`) and stated that all Orders and Finance data is real.
- **What:** one-off migration clears the flag on exactly 8 `orders`, 18 `finance_payment_requests` and 17 `finance_payment_allocations`. Aborts unless Test Data Cleanup is permanently disabled and those exact counts are flagged. Only user triggers on the three tables are off for the single UPDATE (so no activity rows or `updated_at` churn), then back on; it refuses to start if any is already disabled and checks afterwards that all are enabled and that the immutability trigger still refuses a flip. `order_requests` (retired) untouched.
- **Rehearsed** on the linked DB inside BEGIN…ROLLBACK: 43 rows re-labelled, all 43 identical apart from the flag, production still 8/18 flagged afterwards. The dashboard run as admin in the same transaction read the 8 orders.
- **Applied 2026-10-21:** 0 flagged on all three tables, 0 triggers left off, migration recorded. The first push attempt failed harmlessly (LOCK TABLE outside a transaction, the CLI sends statements one by one); the file now wraps itself in begin/commit. The dashboard now counts the 8 orders.
- No rollback by design (the flag evidences a finished phase).

## 2026-10-21 — Orders dashboard: six at-a-glance cards and one action board (PR open) · migration 20270328000000 (APPLIED to production 2026-10-21)
- **Owner request:** the dashboard showed three zero counters and told nobody anything. It now leads with six cards — **Running orders, Ready for production (aligned), Delayed, Payments pending, Fabric or finish pending, Due in 7 days** — each with its rupee value, and below them one **action board**: tabs Delayed / Payments pending / Not aligned / Fabric-finish / Due in 7 days / Ready for production, rows linking to the order (salesperson, due date, value, wait). A card with rows behind it opens that tab. Revenue moved below the board; Factory Focus, document queue and queue pills unchanged.

Same single round trip (startup-shape pin unchanged), same visibility rule, test data still excluded, payment figures only for orders readable without a scope. Delayed = `due_date < today (IST)`. Rollback: re-apply the 20270227000000 definition. Rehearsed on the linked DB inside BEGIN…ROLLBACK (applies, runs as admin, returns the overview); NOT run against real order data because every existing order is test data.
- **Without the migration** the page falls back to the old three-counter layout (overview parses as null), so code can merge first without breaking, but the new view only appears once it is applied.

Turning the test-data phase off is the owner's irreversible decision (`permanently_disable_test_data_cleanup`) and was NOT done here.
- Supersedes the 2026-09 removal of the Overdue card (#154): the owner asked for delayed orders again. `orderDashboardSummary.test.ts` now pins "no overdue" only on the original read and the three attention lists; new `orderDashboardOverview.test.ts` pins the overview.
- Not verified on screen in a signed-in session.

The PI-revision line-matching dialog still receives the old 3-digit label from SQL (20270116/20270120) and re-pads it with `normalizeBoeItemCode`.
